(3.4)

Now if we replace
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})_{\text{bicat}}
with some other bicategory, such as one
(\ast,{\cal D})
coming from a finite linear semisimple category
{\cal D}\in\mathsf{2Vect}^{KV}
, then we obtain finite 2-representations of
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
as studied in
[
62
]
.

\Diamond

The "model change" described in
Remark
3.3
provides a way in which one can treat
(\mathsf{H}\rtimes G)^{\text{edges}}
as 1-cells in
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
. Thus if we truncate/forget the morphisms in
{\cal V}^{X}
, we can extend
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})_{\text{bicat}}
to a category of measureable fields over
(G,\sigma)
, and measureable morphisms parameterized by
\mathsf{H}\rtimes G
. However, in the context of
Definition
3.7
,
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
is treated as a measureable category
externally
over
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
; the categorical structures inherited from the source and target maps in the underlying decorated 1-graphs
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
is
internal
.

The "2-morphisms layer" in
\Lambda
, so to speak, are not populated by the 1-cells in
\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
(ie. the decorated edges
(\mathsf{H}\rtimes G)^{\text{edges}}
), but instead by witnesses for the compatibility of 2-gauge transformations, such as the module associators
\alpha^{\Lambda}_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}:\Lambda_{\zeta}\circ\Lambda_{\zeta^{\prime}}\Rightarrow\Lambda_{\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime}}
. These are measureable natural transformations coming from
secondary gauge transformations

[
59
,
137
,
19
,
1
]
, ie. 2-morphisms
\operatorname{hAd}^{-1}_{\zeta}\Rightarrow\operatorname{hAd}_{\zeta}^{-1}
in the 2-functor 2-groupoid
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}=\operatorname{2Fun}(\Gamma^{2},B\mathbb{G})
over the 2-holonomies, and they are non-invertible when
\mathbb{G}
is a smooth 2-group with weak associators
\tau
.

Remark 3.4
.

A fact in certain theories of 2-representations of 2-groups
\mathbb{G}
is the following:

They only depend on characters of
\mathsf{H}
up to natural isomorphism, even if the 2-group
\mathbb{G}=\mathsf{H}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}G
is not skeletal
\mathsf{t}\neq 0
.

This is known in the following contexts.

•

Kapranov-Voevodsky 2-vector spaces
\mathsf{2Vect}^{KV}

[
30
]
and 2-Hilbert spaces
\mathsf{2Hilb}

[
31
]
(cf.
[
138
,
139
,
40
,
140
,
135
,
141
]
),

•

Crane-Yetter measureable fields (Thm. 49 of
[
117
]
), and

•

Baez-Crans 2-vector spaces
\mathsf{2Vect}^{BC}

[
128
]
(see
[
142
]
).

The first two are of the
enriched
type,
7
7

7

In fact, we will show in §
B
that an ”enriched version” of
Definition
2.4
leads to difficulties, hence defining Hopf categories in this context require some subtle non-obvious constructions
[
24
,
143
]
.
while the last one — though it is of the
internal
type — suffers from the problem that
\mathsf{2Vect}^{BC}
is simply "too strict" to detect any non-trivial
k
-invariants of
\mathbb{G}
. For this, 2-representations over a weaker version of Baez-Crans 2-vector spaces (ie. one enriched in bimodules of
A_{\infty}
-algebras, instead of strictly associative differential graded algebras) was studied in
[
144
]
.

\Diamond

The 2-gauge transformations
\Lambda

must
have this "2-morphisms layer" in the context of weak 2-Chern-Simons theory; see also
[
62
]
and
Remark
2.2
. However, when the Lie 2-group is strict, such 2-morphisms can be truncated.

3.1.3
Locality of states and gauge transformations

Now a crucial feature of any lattice gauge theory is
locality.
This is the notion that the data attached to the lattice, be it states or gauge transformations, should commute if they have disjoint support. In order to express this notion, we first define the so-called localized states and 2-gauge transformations.

Definition 3.8
.

Let
(e,f)=e\xrightarrow{f}e_{f}\in\Gamma^{2}
denote a 2-graph with source edge
e
. The
2-graph state localized at
(e,f)
corresponding to
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
is defined by the measureable field
\phi_{(e,f)}
whose stalk Hilbert spaces are given by

(\phi_{(e,f)})_{\{(h_{e^{\prime}},b_{f^{\prime}})\}_{(e^{\prime},f^{\prime})}}=\chi^{[2]}_{(e,f)}\phi_{\{(e^{\prime},f^{\prime})\}_{(e^{\prime},f^{\prime})}},

where
\chi^{[2]}_{(e,f)}
is the characteristic measure on
{\Gamma^{2}}
supported at the face
{(e,f)}
. As a sheaf of smooth sections,
\phi_{(e,f)}
is the restriction sheaf of
\phi
along the inclusion
(e,f)\hookrightarrow\Gamma^{2}
.

More precisely, the restriction sheaf is the direct image of the induced pullback
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}^{(e,f)}
.

With these localized 2-graphs states, the geometry of the 2-graphs become apparent. If we let
\Delta
denote the pullback measureable field of (group/groupoid) multiplication
\cdot_{h,v}
in
\mathbb{G}
, such that we have, in Sweedler notation, an isomorphism of stalks

(-\otimes-)\Delta(\phi)_{\mathrm{z},\mathrm{z}^{\prime}}=\bigoplus(\phi_{(1)}^{h,v})_{\mathrm{z}}\otimes(\phi_{(2)}^{h,v})_{\mathrm{z}^{\prime}}\cong\phi_{\mathrm{z}\cdot_{h,v}\mathrm{z}^{\prime}},\qquad\mathrm{z},\mathrm{z^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{G}

for all
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G})
, then we can promote this coproduct to
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}
in accordance with the geometry:

\Delta_{h,v}(\phi_{(e,f)})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\bigoplus(\phi_{(1)}^{h,v})_{(e_{1},f_{1})}\times(\phi_{(2)}^{h,v})_{(e_{2},f_{2})},&;(e,f)=(e_{1},f_{1})\cup_{h,v}(e_{2},f_{2})\\
\phi_{(e_{1},f_{1})}\times\phi_{(e_{2},f_{2})}&;(e_{1},f_{1})\cap(e_{2},f_{2})=\emptyset\end{cases}

where
\cup_{h,v}
are horizontal/vertical 2-graph gluing laws displayed in fig.
1
. In the case where the 2-graphs
(e_{1},f_{1}),(e_{2},f_{2})
are disjoint,
(e,f)
is interpreted as their disjoint union and the coproduct is grouplike/cocommutative.

Figure 1:
The two ways in which a local 2-graph
(e,f)
can be decomposed into two 2-graphs, depending on how
(e,f)
intersects an embedded 2-cell
C
in the 3d manifold
\Sigma
. The left denotes
\cup_{h},
in which the normal vectors of
C
are locally tangent to the source edge
e
of
f
around
v=s(e)
, while the right denotes
\cup_{v}
, where the normal vectors are perpendicular to
e
.

We emphasize here that there are
two
coproduct operations hidden in the symbol "
\Delta
", which correspond to the horizontal or the vertical labels
h,v
. These coproducts are required to satisfy the cointerchange law

(\Delta_{h}\times\Delta_{h})\circ\Delta_{v}\cong(1\times\sigma\times 1)\circ(\Delta_{v}\times\Delta_{v})\Delta_{h}

on
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
, which can be seen to arise from the geometry of
triple intersections
of 2-cells in
\Sigma
. We shall in the following abbreviate
\Delta_{h,v}
as
\Delta
when no confusion is possible; explicit details can be found in
[
1
]
.

Similarly to for the 2-gauge transformations, it also inherits its notion of locality from the underlying geometry, this time of the
1-graphs
. Like the 2-graphs states, this is captured by the coproducts
\tilde{\Delta}
on
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
.

Definition 3.9
.

Let
(v,e)=v\xrightarrow{e}v_{f}\in\Gamma^{1}
denote a 1-graph with source vertex
v
. The
2-gauge transformation localized at
(v,e)
corresponding to
\Lambda
is a norm-smooth assignment

\zeta\mapsto\chi_{(v,e)}^{[1]}\Lambda_{\zeta},\qquad\zeta\in\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}

of measureable direct image endofunctors on
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
, where
\chi^{[1]}_{(v,e)}
is the characteristic measure on
{\Gamma^{1}}
supported at the edge
{(v,e)}
.

The way local 2-gauge transformations stack geometrically are dictated by the coproducts
\tilde{\Delta}
,

\Lambda_{(\tilde{\Delta}_{h,v})_{\zeta}}^{(v,e)}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\bigoplus\big(\Lambda_{\zeta_{(1)}}^{(v_{1},e_{1})}\big)^{h,v}\times\big(\Lambda_{\zeta_{(2)}}^{(v_{2},e_{2})}\big)^{h,v}&;(v,e)=(v_{1},e_{1})\cup_{h,v}(v_{2},e_{2}),\\
\Lambda^{(v_{1},e_{1})}_{\zeta}\times\Lambda^{(v_{2},e_{2})}_{\zeta}&;(v_{1},e_{1})\cap(v_{2},e_{2})=\emptyset\end{cases}

where
\cup_{v}
results in the composition
v_{1}\xrightarrow{e_{1}}(v_{1})_{f}=v_{2}\xrightarrow{e_{2}}(v_{2})_{f}
and
\cup_{h}
is a PL identification
(v_{1},e_{1})\xrightarrow{\sim}(v_{2},e_{2})
. As in the case of the 2-graph states, when
(v_{1},e_{1}),(v_{2},e_{2})
are disjoint then
(v,e)
is interpreted as their disjoint union.

Given the above coproduct structures, which are based intrinsically in the geometry of the 2d lattice
\Gamma
, we can now state the central characterization theorem proven in
[
1
]
.

Theorem 3.5
.

Let
{\bf C}=\mathsf{LieGrp}\subset\mathsf{Mfld}
denote the site of Lie groups.

•

The 2-graph states
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\in\mathsf{Hopf}(\operatorname{Cocat}_{{\cal V}^{\bf C}})
is a symmetric Hopf cocategory internal to the measureable sheaves
{\cal V}^{\bf C}
of Hermitian sections.

•

The 2-gauge parameters
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}\in\mathsf{Hopf}(\operatorname{Cat}_{{\cal V}^{\bf C}})
is a cosymmetric Hopf category internal to the measureable sheaves
{\cal V}^{\bf C}
of bounded linear operators.

Here, cosymmetric means the coproduct is cocommutative up to invertible higher morphisms.

In fact,
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
is a
regular
representation category over
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
, equipped with a bimodule structure
\bullet
such that

\zeta^{-1}\bullet\phi\bullet\zeta=\Lambda_{\zeta}\phi,\qquad\forall\penalty\ \phi\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}),\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}.

There is a natural module associator natural isomorphism

(\alpha^{\bullet})_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}^{\phi}:\phi\bullet(\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\phi\bullet\zeta)\bullet\zeta^{\prime}

induced from the strict associativity of
\mathbb{G}
.

Figure 2:
The graphical representation of the derivation property (
3.5
), which implements the geometric consitency between the product
\otimes
and the 2-gauge transformation action
\Lambda
.

There are also sheaf automorphisms witnessing the following "derivation property":

\otimes\big((\phi\times\phi^{\prime})\bullet{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}}\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\phi\otimes\phi^{\prime})\bullet\zeta,

(3.5)

where
\otimes
is the monoidal structure on
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
and
\tilde{\Delta}
is the coproduct functor on
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
. See also fig.
2
. Algebraically, (
3.5
) implies that
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
is a monoidal
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
-module category.

Remark 3.5
.

As suggested by its notation "
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
", (
3.5
) is a condition shared by the universal enveloping algebra. Indeed, one categorical level down, this condition

(\psi\psi^{\prime})\bullet\zeta=\mu\big((\psi\otimes\psi^{\prime})\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}\big)=(\psi\zeta)\psi^{\prime}+\psi(\psi^{\prime}\zeta)

is precisely the Leibniz rule for the derivation action of
\zeta\in U\mathfrak{g}
on functions
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in C(G)
of a compact Lie group
G
.

\Diamond

This observation led to the following definitions.

Definition 3.10
.

Suppose
\Gamma
is a single PL 2-disc, consisting of a single face bounded by an edge loop based at a vertex.

1.

We call
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})=\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G})
the
categorical coordinate ring
of
\mathbb{G}
.

2.

We call
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}=\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}
the
categorical universal enveloping algebra
of
\mathfrak{G}
.

We emphasize here that this name and notation for
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}
is just suggestive; see also
Remark
3.7
later. While
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G})
was concretely constructed as a category of sheaves,
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}
may be an example of the so-called
manifold tensor category

[
145
]
.

In the following, we will recall the quantum deformation of these structures introduced by the 2-Chern-Simons theory.

3.2
Deformation quantization and the combinatorial 2-Fock-Rosly bracket

Let us now briefly recall the procedure for deformation quantizing
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
. From the classical 2-Chern-Simons action
S_{2CS}
, one can extract the presymplectic form
\omega
as well as the Lie 2-algebra cobracket
\delta
. The coefficients of these data, as in the usual Chern-Simons theory
[
23
]
, combine to give a classical 2-graded
r
-matrix
[
7
,
8
]
of degree-1

(1\otimes\mu_{1})r=(\mu_{1}\otimes 1)r,\qquad r\sim\omega+\delta\in(\mathfrak{G}^{\otimes 2})_{1}.

It is known
[
146
]
that the semiclassical symmetries of 2-Chern-Simons theory is captured by the Lie 2-bialgebra
(\mathfrak{G};\delta)
determined by this classical 2-
r
-matrix.

We now leverage the main result in
[
5
]
.

Theorem 3.6
.

There is a one-to-one correspondence between Lie 2-bialgebras and
Poisson-Lie 2-groups

(\mathbb{G};\Pi)
, which are Lie 2-groups
\mathbb{G}
equipped with a multiplicative bivector field
\Pi\in\mathfrak{X}^{2}(\mathbb{G})
.

Elements of the universal envelope of
\mathfrak{G}
, such as the classical 2-
r
-matrix
r
, act on functions of
\mathbb{G}
by graded derivations
[
5
]
.

This induces a 2-graded Poisson bracket
\{-,-\}

[
5
,
8
]
which gives rise to the following
combinatorial 2-Fock-Rosly Poisson brackets
(here
\hbar=\frac{2\pi}{k}
)

\displaystyle\{f_{(e_{1},f_{1})},f_{(e_{2},f_{2})}\}

\displaystyle=\hbar\big({\delta_{t(e),s(e^{\prime})}}{r(f_{(e_{1},f_{1})}\cdot f_{(e_{2},f_{2})})}-{\delta_{s(e),t(e^{\prime})}}{(f_{(e_{1},f_{1})}\cdot f_{(e_{2},f_{2})})r^{T}}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\hbar\big((-\cdot-)[r,\Delta_{\mathrm{h}}(\phi_{(e,f)})]_{c}\big)

on localized functions
f_{(e,f)}\in C(X)
of the decorated 2-graphs
X=\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}
. Here,
(e,f)=(e_{1},f_{1})\cup_{\mathrm{h}}(e_{2},f_{2})
denotes the 2-graph obtained from
gluing

(e_{1},f_{1})
with
(e_{2},f_{2})
such that
e=e_{1}\ast e_{2}
or
e=e_{2}\ast e_{1}
(ie. the
source edges
are composed).

3.2.1
Quantum 2-graph states

We now invoke the central result in
[
147
]
: for each smooth Riemannian manifold
X
and a fixed
\star
-product on the
C^{*}
-algebra
C(X)
, there is a unique (up to isometry)
\star
-product on the smooth sections
\Gamma(E)
of a Hermitian vector bundle
E\rightarrow X
, treated as sheaves of
C(X)
-modules over the ring of power series in
\hbar=\frac{2\pi}{k}
. We denote such sheaves by
\Gamma(E)[[\hbar]]
.

As such, the
\star
-product on
C(X)
, obtained from the deformation quantization along the Fock-Rosly 2-group Poisson bracket
\{-,-\}
above, extends to sections
\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})
of
any
measureable Hermitian vector bundle
H^{X}\rightarrow X
over
X
. This extension, in particular, satisfies the following
semiclassical limit

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\hbar}\big(\xi\star\xi^{\prime}-\xi\star\xi\big)=\{\xi,\xi^{\prime}\},

where
\xi,\xi^{\prime}
are sections in the
same
sheaf
\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})
.

Moreover, this deformation quantization also determines a
\star
-product on sections of the tensor product sheaf
\big(\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})\otimes\Gamma_{c}(H^{\prime X})\big)[[\hbar]]\cong\Gamma_{c}((H\otimes H^{\prime})^{X})[[\hbar]]
. This allows us to define a
tensor
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}
-product
, as a deformation the usual symmetric tensor product
\otimes
, equipped with sheaf automorphisms

\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})[[\hbar]]\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Gamma_{c}(H^{\prime X})[[\hbar]]\cong\Gamma_{c}((H\otimes H^{\prime})^{X})[[\hbar]]

(3.6)

over the ring of formal power series in
\hbar
. This deformed tensor product then by construction satisfies the following
Dirac quantization condition
: formally, for each
\phi=\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})[[\hbar]],\phi^{\prime}=\Gamma_{c}(H^{\prime X})[[\hbar]]\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
, we have a sheaf automorphism on
\Gamma_{c}((H\otimes H^{\prime})^{X})[[\hbar]]
on which

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{i\hbar}\big(\xi\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\xi^{\prime}-\xi^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\xi\big)\mapsto\{\xi,\xi^{\prime}\},

(3.7)

with respect to the combinatorial 2-group Fock-Rosly Poisson bracket, for sections
\xi\in\phi,\penalty\ \xi^{\prime}\in\phi^{\prime}
on
different
sheaves.

Definition 3.11
.

Let
q=e^{i\hbar}=e^{i\frac{2\pi}{k}}
and
X=(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}},\mu_{\Gamma^{2}})
. Denote by
{\cal V}^{X}_{q}\subset{\cal H}^{X}
the category of meaureable sheaves of
Hermitian
\hbar
-power series sections

\phi=\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})[[\hbar]]
— namely,
\phi
is a local finitely-generated projective Hilbert
C(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]
-module. The morphisms are measureable essentially bounded
\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]
-linear operators.

The associated tensor
\star
-product
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}
(
3.6
) defines a monoidal functor
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}:{\cal V}^{X}_{q}\times{\cal V}^{X}_{q}\rightarrow{\cal V}^{X}_{q}
satisfying (
3.7
) (and fit into (
3.10
) later). This makes
{\cal V}^{X}_{q}\in\mathsf{Meas}
into a non-commutative algebra object in
\mathsf{Meas}
.

The
quantum 2-graph states

\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\subset{\cal V}_{q}^{X}
on
X=(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}},\mu_{\Gamma^{2}})
is the full monoidal 2-subcategory whose norm-completions
\Gamma(H^{X})[[\hbar]]
are
separable
: namely they define sheaves of countably-generated Hilbert
L^{2}(X,\mu_{\Gamma^{2}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]
-modules over
X
.

These quantum 2-graph states
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
are precisely those which underlie the discrete degrees-of-freedom in quantum 2-Chern-Simons theory.

Recall that elements of the universal envelope of
\mathfrak{G}
acts on
C(\mathbb{G})
, and hence sections on
\mathbb{G}
, by derivations.

Theorem 3.7
.

The quantum 2-graph states
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
is a (non-symmetric) Hopf cocategory in
{\cal V}^{\bf C}
equipped with a
cobraiding
natural transformation

R:\Delta_{h}\Rightarrow\Delta^{\text{op}}_{h},

where each component
R_{\phi}
at
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
is a conjugation implementing a measureable morphism of sheaves,

R_{\phi}:R\Big(\sum\xi_{(1)}\times\xi_{(2)}\Big)R^{-1}\mapsto\sum\xi_{(2)}\times\xi_{(1)},\qquad\sum\xi_{(1)}\times\xi_{(2)}\in\Delta_{h}(\phi),

(3.8)

via the 2-
R
-matrix
R_{\phi}\sim 1+i\hbar r+o((i\hbar)^{2})
.

Of course, being natural means
R
is compatible with the interchange law for
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}
, and being monoidal means
R
satisfies the quasitriangularity axioms.

We emphasize once again from
Remark
2.2
, in the current case of the
strict
2-Chern-Simons theory (ie. in the absence of the weak associator
\tau
), the Hopf structures are strict with invertible coherence morphisms witnessing them.

3.2.2
Quantum 2-gauge transformations

Upon quantization, the 2-gauge transformations must also deformed accordingly. We shall do this indirectly by preserving certain consistency conditions under the new
\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]
-module structure aforded by deformation quantization.

More precisely, it was proven in
[
1
]
that, if
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
is to remain a
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
-module category satisfying the property (
3.5
), then
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
must itself inherit a non-trivial 2-
R
-matrix and a quantum deformed
coproduct

\tilde{\Delta}
satisfying

\displaystyle R\otimes\big(\Lambda_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}}(\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2})\big)\cong\Lambda_{\tilde{R}\cdot\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}}(\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2})

\displaystyle\big(\Lambda_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}}(\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2})\big)\otimes R\cong\Lambda_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}\cdot\tilde{R}}(\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2}).

(3.9)

As a result,
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
becomes a Hopf algebroid which is non-cosymmetric.

Remark 3.6
.

Note that, due to the locality nature of the 2-gauge symmetry §
3.1.3
, these "new" operators
\tilde{R}
induced by (
3.9
) are localized on the 1-skeleton
\Gamma^{1}
. We will prove in §
4.4
that they are in fact localized on the boundary 1-skeleton
\partial\Gamma^{2}
of the 2-skeleton
\Gamma^{2}
on which the 2-graph states live.

\Diamond

Theorem 3.8
.

Let
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
denote the 2-gauge transformation parameters equipped with a cobraiding
\tilde{R}:\tilde{\Delta}\Rightarrow\tilde{\Delta}^{\text{op}}
, then it is a (non-cosymmetric) Hopf category in
{\cal V}^{\bf C}
. Moreover, if
\tilde{R}
satisfies (
3.9
), then the derivation property (
3.5
) extends to the
\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]
-linear, quantum setting on
{\cal V}^{X}_{q}
.

In accordance with the above, we can now introduce the categorical versions of compact quantum groups, in analogy to the quantum coordinate rings of Woronowicz
[
22
]
or the quantum enveloping algebras of Drinfel’d-Jimbo
[
148
,
149
]
.

Definition 3.12
.

Suppose
\Gamma
is a PL 2-disc.

1.

\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})=\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G})
is called the
quantum categorical coordinate ring
.

2.

\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}=\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
is called the
quantum categorical enveloping algebra
.

Remark 3.7
.

Again, the notation "
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
" should be taken as suggestive, since we have described its quantum algebraic structures in a somewhat indirect way. We conjecture that there should be a more direct approach to defining
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
, which categorifies the Drinfel’d-Jimbo deformation quantization.

\Diamond

3.2.3
The semiclassical limit

Recall from
Definition
3.11
and the results of
[
147
]
that there is a forgetful functor
{\cal V}^{X}_{q}\rightarrow{\cal V}^{X}
which sends
\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})[[\hbar]]\mapsto\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})
by "evaluating" at
\hbar=0
. This functor renders the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\times\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\star$\cr}}{\vtop{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\textstyle\star$\cr}}{\vtop{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}{\vtop{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}

(3.10)

commutative, up to the homotopy given by the sheaf automorphism (
3.6
).

Through the following known results,

1.

2-term Hopf
A_{\infty}
-algebras with a 2-
R
-matrix admit Lie 2-bialgebras with a classical
r
-matrix as a limit (Appendix B,
[
144
]
),

2.

there is a one-to-one correspondence between Lie 2-bialgebras
[
7
]
and connected, simply-connected Poisson-Lie 2-groups
[
5
]
,

the following semiclassical limit theorem was proven in
[
62
]
under a certain hypothesis. This hypothesis posits the existence of a certain "decategorification functor"
8
8

8

We will say more about this in
Remark
A.1
.

\lambda:\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G})
which preserves all of the Hopf algebraic properties.

Theorem 3.9
.

Under a certain hypothesis,
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G})
reduces to a Poisson-Lie 2-group
\mathbb{G}
in the semiclassical limit. Moreover, the corresponding Lie 2-bialgebra
(\mathfrak{G};\delta)
is the one governing the symmetries of the 2-Chern-Simons action.

It is in this sense that the quantum 2-graph states quantize the semiclassical degrees-of-freedom in 2-Chern-Simons theory.

This also pins
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G})
down a categorical analogue of the quantum coordinate ring (cf.
[
22
,
150
]
), which was used crucially in the seminal work of Witten
[
151
]
relating observables in Chern-Simons theory to representations of
U_{q}\mathfrak{g}
.

As mentioned in
Remark
3.7
, it is reasonable to expect a parallel, categorical analogue of the Drinfel’d-Jimbo construction for
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
, as well as a categorical analogue of the quantum Fourier theory
[
152
]
which ties them together. We will say a bit more about this in the conclusion.

3.3
The Lattice 2-algebra

Equipped with the above structures,
[
1
]
defined the
lattice 2-algebra
of 2-Chern-Simons theory. It is endowed with certain conditions which are categorical analogues of those in the lattice algebra for Chern-Simons theory
[
23
]
.

Definition 3.13
.

The
lattice 2-algebra

\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma}
for 2-Chern-Simons theory on the lattice
\Gamma
is the monoidal semidirect product (cf.
[
153
]
)
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\rtimes\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
through the right action
\bullet
, such that each
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
satisfy:

1.

the
left-covariance
condition

\phi\bullet(a_{v},\gamma_{e})\cong(1\otimes\Lambda)_{\tilde{\Delta}(a_{v},\gamma_{e})}\bullet\phi,\qquad\forall\penalty\ (a_{v},\gamma_{e})\in\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}

(3.11)

2.

on local 2-graph states, there exist sheaf isomorphism witnessing the
braid relations

\phi_{(e,f)}\times\phi_{(e^{\prime},f^{\prime})}\cong\begin{cases}\phi_{(e^{\prime},f^{\prime})}\times\phi_{(e,f)}&;(e,f)\cap(e^{\prime},f^{\prime})=\emptyset\\
(\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{R}_{e}}(\phi_{(e^{\prime},f^{\prime})}\times\phi_{(e,f)})&;e\cup\partial f^{\prime}\neq\emptyset\end{cases},

(3.12)

where
\tilde{R}_{e}
is the 2-
R
-matrix of
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}=\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{(v,e)}
localized on the edge
e
.

The braid relations ensure that both sides of (
3.12
) furnish the same
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
-representation, up to intertwining homotopy. They can in fact be deduced from the commutativity properties of 2-graph states as well as (
3.9
).

The derivation property (
3.5
) is crucial in this construction, as the underlying sheaf automorphism gives precisely a coherent monoidal module tensorator

\displaystyle\varpi:(-\bullet-)\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(-\bullet-)\Rightarrow(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\bullet-,

\displaystyle\varpi^{\phi,\phi^{\prime}}_{\zeta}:\sum(\phi\bullet\zeta_{(1)})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(\phi^{\prime}\bullet\zeta_{(2)})=(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\big((\phi\times\phi^{\prime})\bullet\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\phi\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi^{\prime})\bullet\zeta.

It is worth mentioning here that the natural sheaf isomorphism witnessing the braid relations (
3.12
) is obtained from the cobraiding structures
R,\tilde{R}
on
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}),\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
(
3.8
), (
3.9
), as well as the tensorator (
3.3
).

3.3.1
2-Chern-Simons lattice observables

In a field theory, from the purely algebraic perspective, observables should be defined as the "gauge invariants" — in an appropriate sense — of all possible configurations. This philosophy takes different guises in different physical contexts: such as in the invertible TQFT context
[
154
]
and in the perturbative QFT context
[
52
]
.

In our case in the context of the 2-category
\mathsf{Meas}
, this idea takes the form of the following explicit definition.

Definition 3.14
.

The
observables of 2-Chern-Simons theory

\mathcal{O}^{\Gamma}
consist of those 2-graph states
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
equipped with natural measureable sheaf isomorphisms

\phi\bullet\zeta\cong\zeta\bullet\phi,\qquad\forall\penalty\ \zeta\in A,

(3.53)

witnessing the
invariance condition
, where
A\subset\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
runs over all Borel measureable subsets. By construction, there is a fully-faithful internal functor
\mathcal{O}^{\Gamma}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma}
into the lattice 2-algebra.

By (
3.11
), the observables
\mathcal{O}^{\Gamma}
are equivalently those 2-graph states
\phi
which are equipped with measureable natural isomorphisms (
3.53
)
\Lambda_{\zeta}\phi\cong\phi
for all
\zeta\in\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
.

In other words,
\mathcal{O}^{\Gamma}=\big(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\big)^{\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}}
are the
homotopy fixed-points
.
9
9

9

Under the model change described in
Remark
3.3
, the observables is the
equivariantization
of
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
under the 2-gauge transformations
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
, with respect to the module structure (
3.4
).
This is a categorical analogue of the Chern-Simons observables defined in
[
23
]
— as invariants of the algebra of observables.

Remark 3.8
.

Suppose the PL 2-manifold
S
, embedded in a 3d manifold
\Sigma
, has two triangulations
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
that are refinements of each other — that is, there is an embedding
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\supset\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime}
of their corresponding simplicial complexes — then there is a monoidal restriction functor of sheaves
f_{\Gamma\supset\Gamma^{\prime}}:\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
on the lattice 2-algebras. The family
\big(\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma},f_{\Gamma\supset\Gamma^{\prime}}\big)_{\Gamma}
thus forms a direct system in the double bicategory of cobraided Hopf cocategories
{\cal A}=\operatorname{cobHopf}_{{\cal V}^{\bf C}}
in
{\cal V}^{\bf C}
, where
{\bf C}=\mathsf{LieGrp}
. If 2-colimits exist in
{\cal A}
, then we can take the direct limit to obtain the "universal" 2-Chern-Simons algebra
\mathcal{B}=\lim_{\Gamma\rightarrow}\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma}
.

\Diamond

Remark 3.9
.

Since each
\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma}
is a monoidal semidirect product and each functor
f_{\Gamma\supset\Gamma^{\prime}}
is monoidal,
\mathcal{B}
can also be written as a monoidal semidirect product
\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{q}(\mathbb{G})\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{U}}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
(these may not coincide on-the-nose with
Definition
3.12
). The homotopy fixed points
\mathcal{O}=(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{q}(\mathbb{G}))^{\overline{\mathbb{U}}_{q}\mathfrak{G}}
would then, analogous to the lattice algebra in Chern-Simons theory
[
23
]
, be able to be interpreted as a model for the
quantum categorified moduli space of flat 2-connections.

\Diamond

3.3.2
2-
\dagger
unitarity of the 2-holonomies

Recall in the above theorems that
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
is a
Hopf
cocategory, which has equipped a antipode functor. Similar to the coproducts
\Delta,
these antipode functors
S
are intimately tied to the geometry of the underlying 2-graphs. Specifically,
S
is induced from
orientation reversal
.

Following
Example 5.5
of
[
155
]
, we take the embedded graph
\Gamma\subset\Sigma
as a framed piecewise-linear (PL) 2-manifold, then the PL-group
\operatorname{PL}(2)=O(2)=SO(2)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
tells us directly what the 2-dagger structure on
\Gamma
is —
\dagger_{2}
is given by the orientation reversal
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup and
\dagger_{1}
is a
2\pi
-rotation in framing
SO(2)
-factor.

Crucially, these daggers are involutive
\dagger_{2}^{2}=\operatorname{id},\penalty\ \dagger^{2}_{1}\cong\operatorname{id}
and they
strongly commute

\dagger_{2}\circ\dagger_{1}=\dagger_{1}^{\text{op}}\circ\dagger_{2}.

(3.54)

For edges in
\Gamma^{1}
, on the other hand,
\dagger_{2}
implements an orientatino reversal
e^{\dagger_{2}}=\bar{e}
while
\dagger_{1}
rotates its framing: if
\nu
is a trivialization of the normal bundle along the embedding
e\hookrightarrow\Sigma
, then
(e,\nu)^{\dagger_{1}}=(e,-\nu)
. Let us denote this frame rotation by the shorthand
e^{T}=(e,-\nu)
.

We denote the induced maps on the measureable Lie 2-groups by

X=\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{X}^{\mathrm{h,v}}=\mathbb{G}^{(\Gamma^{2})^{\dagger_{2},\dagger_{1}}}
.

Definition 3.15
.

The
2-
\dagger
unitarity of the 2-holonomies
is the property that:

•

For each 2-graph states
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
, we have stalk-wise for each
\mathrm{z}=\{(h_{e},b_{f})\}_{(e,f)}\in\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}
,

\displaystyle(S_{h}\phi)_{\mathrm{z}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\phi}_{\mathrm{z}^{\dagger_{1}}},\qquad\mathrm{z}^{\dagger_{1}}=\{(h_{e^{\dagger_{1}}},b_{f^{\dagger_{1}}})\}_{(e,f)}

\displaystyle(S_{v}\phi)_{\mathrm{z}}

\displaystyle=\phi^{T}_{\mathrm{z}^{\dagger_{2}}},\qquad\mathrm{z}^{\dagger_{2}}=\{(h_{e^{\dagger_{2}}},b_{f^{\dagger_{2}}})\}_{(e,f)}

where
\bar{\phi}
is the measureable field
(H^{*})^{X}
complex linear dual to
\phi
, and
\phi^{T}
is the same sheaf underlying
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
but equipped with the adjoint sheaf morphisms.

•

For the 2-gauge transformations
\Lambda:\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
, we have pointwise for each
\zeta=\{(a_{v},\gamma_{e}\}_{(e,v)}\in\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
(recall
e^{T}=(e,-\nu)
denotes a frame rotation of an edge),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\tilde{S}_{h}\zeta}

\displaystyle=\bar{\Lambda}_{\zeta^{\dagger_{1}}},\qquad\zeta^{\dagger_{1}}=\{(a_{v^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\bar{e}}}a_{v})\}_{(a,v)},

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\tilde{S}_{v}\zeta}

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{T}_{\zeta^{\dagger_{2}}},\qquad\zeta^{\dagger_{1}}=\{(a_{v}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{e^{T}}}a_{v^{\prime}})\}_{(a,v)}

where
\bar{\Lambda}_{\zeta}
is the complex conjugate measureable functor, and
\Lambda_{\zeta}^{T}
is the adjoint functor.

Note for
C=\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
, the vertical antipode
S_{v}:C\rightarrow C^{\bar{\text{op}},\text{c-op}_{v}}
reverses both the
external
(ie. in
\mathsf{Meas}_{X}
) composition and the internal (ie. in
C_{1}
) cocomoposition
\Delta_{v}
. On the other hand, the horizontal antipode
S_{h}:C\rightarrow C^{\text{m-op,c-op}_{h}}
is internally op-
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}
-monoidal and op-comonoidal.

Remark 3.10
.

The
\dagger
-unitarity property intertwines the external
\dagger
-adjoint structures and the internal geometry of the underlying 2-graph
\Gamma
. In the undeformed case, the involutive
\dagger
-structures on
\Gamma
pushes forward to an involutive equivalence on the sheaves
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{(\Gamma^{2})^{\dagger_{1,2}}})
, whence
\dagger
-unitarity forces the antipodes in the undeformed case to themselves also be involutive. However, this is not the case in the quantum deformed case. Though the dual
\bar{\cdot}
remains involutive, the antipode
S_{h}
generally is not in the quantum theory. There is no homotopy witnessesing the commutativity between the
\dagger_{1}
-functor and the classical limit §
3.2.3
, unless certain conditions on the cobraiding
R
is satisfied.

\Diamond

3.3.3
*-operations

Denote by
\eta_{\mathrm{h,v}}:\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})[[\hbar]]\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(H^{\overline{X}^{\mathrm{h,v}}})[[\hbar]]
the
\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]
-linear measureable sheaf morphisms induced on the 2-graph states by the 2-
\dagger
structure of
\Gamma^{2}
.

Definition 3.16
.

We say the pair
(\eta_{\mathrm{h}},\eta_{\mathrm{v}})
is a
2-
\dagger
-intertwining pair
iff for each
\zeta\in\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
, we have

\eta_{\mathrm{h}}\big(\Lambda_{\zeta}\phi_{(e,f)}\big)=\Lambda_{\bar{\zeta}}(\eta_{\mathrm{h}}\phi)_{(\bar{e}^{\prime},\bar{f})},\qquad\eta_{\mathrm{v}}\big(\Lambda_{\zeta}\phi_{(e,f)}\big)=\Lambda_{\zeta}(\eta_{\mathrm{v}}\phi)_{(e^{\prime},\bar{f})}

as operators on each quantum 2-graph state
\phi=\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})[[\hbar]]\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
, where
U_{\zeta}
denotes the field of bounded invertible operators realizing the 2-gauge transformations
\Lambda_{\zeta}
.

We are finally ready to state the *-operations on the 2-graph states and the 2-gauge transformations. Suppose the
R
-matrix
\tilde{R}
on
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
is invertible, in the sense that the induced cobraiding natural transformations
\tilde{\Delta}\Rightarrow\tilde{\Delta}^{\text{op}}
are invertible.

Due to the locality properties §
3.1.3
, it suffice to define the *-operations on local pieces.

Definition 3.17
.

Let
(v,e)=v\xrightarrow{e}v^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{1}
denote a 1-graph. The
*-operations
on localized elements in
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
are given by

\zeta_{(v,e)}^{*_{2}}=\zeta_{(v^{\prime},\bar{e})},\qquad\zeta^{*_{1}}_{(v,e)}=\zeta_{(v,e)^{T}}

(3.55)

where
v^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\bar{e}}v
is the orientation-reversal and
v\xrightarrow{e^{T}}v^{\prime}
is the framing rotation.

Let
(e,f)\in\Gamma^{2}
denote a 2-graph with source and target edges
e,e^{\prime}:v\rightarrow v^{\prime}
. The
*-operations
on localized 2-graph states
\phi_{(e,f)}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
are given by

\displaystyle\phi_{(e,f)}^{*_{1}}=(\Lambda\otimes 1)_{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{h}}^{-1}}(\phi_{(\bar{e}^{\prime},\bar{f})})\eta_{\mathrm{h}},

\displaystyle\phi_{(e,f)}^{*_{2}}=(\Lambda\otimes 1)_{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{v}}^{-1}}(\phi_{(e^{\prime},\bar{f})})\eta_{\mathrm{v}},

where
(\bar{e}^{\prime},\bar{f})=(e,f)^{\dagger_{1}}
and
(e^{\prime},\bar{f})=(e,f)^{\dagger_{2}}
, and the relevant
\tilde{R}
-matrices are localized on
\partial f
.

Note crucially that these *-operations are in general
not
involutive.

A routine check yields the following
[
1
]
.

Proposition 3.10
.

The *-operations strongly commute,
(-^{*_{1}})^{\text{op}}\circ-^{*_{2}}\cong(-^{*_{2}})^{\text{m-op,c-op}}\circ-^{*_{1}}
.

Throughout the following, we will assume that both
-^{*_{1}},-^{*_{2}}
are equivalences of measureable categories, with
-^{*_{2}}
is idempotent/involutive but
-^{*_{1}}
not necessarily (unless
q=1
; see §7,
[
62
]
).

Remark 3.11
.

We pause here to note that the definition (
3.55
) essentially states that a frame reversal
(e,\nu)\mapsto(e,-\nu)
on a 1-graph is implemented by the antipode on the decorations. This is an important fact for gluing localized 2-graphs: the interfacing edge has opposite framing depending on which local 2-graph it is embedded into.

\Diamond

To extend the above definition globally to the entire lattice configuration on
\Gamma
, the following was proven in
[
1
]
.

Theorem 3.11
.

Given 2-
\dagger
-unitarity holds on each quantum 2-graphs state, the *-operations preserves (i) the
\bullet
-bimodule structure
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\circlearrowleft\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
, (ii) the covariance condition (
3.11
), and (iii) the braiding relations (
3.12
). Thus they extend to the lattice 2-algebra
\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma}
.

In fact, under the unitarity property defined above, the compatibility of the *-operations with (
3.11
), (
3.12
) is
equivalent
to the various axioms satisfied by the antipode/cobraiding
\tilde{S},\tilde{R}
on
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
.

For instance, the fact that
*_{1}
preserves (
3.12
) is equivalent to the quasitriangularity of
\tilde{R}
/comonoidality of the cobraiding; an analogous fact is true also for the lattice algebra in the usual 3d Chern-Simons theory
[
23
]
. As such, the fact that
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
forms a
strict cobraided Hopf *-category
in
{\cal V}_{q}
can be completely deduced from the *-operations on
\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma}
.

4
Higher-algebra of dense 2-holonomies/2-monodromies

We now formally begin the main contents of this paper. Given the underlying 2d lattice
\Gamma
, we model its triangulation as a simplicial complex. Its 2-truncation
\Gamma^{2}
is a
2-graph
, whose
2
-groupoid structure describes how the closed 2-simplices are glued together in
\Gamma^{2}
. Using this idea, we seek to build 2-graph states
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
from the local quantum categorified coordinate ring
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G})\simeq\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta^{2}})
living on each fundamental 2-simplex
\Delta^{2}=\Delta
.

4.1
Setting up the 2-simplex geometry

We shall label a fundamental 2-simplex by specifying its edges and face
({\bf e}=(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}),f)
, such that the 2-holonomy decorations satisfy fake-flatness
t(b_{f})=h_{\partial f}
with
\partial f=e_{1}-e_{2}+e_{3}
. We will in the following identify the first edge
e_{1}
as the
source edge
of the face
f
. Once this choice is made, the cyclic ordering of the vertices and the rest of the edges are induced by the orientation of the face
f
in
\Delta
.

Consider an embedded triangulated 2-manifold
\Gamma\subset\Sigma^{3}
. Its vertex, edge and face ordering is inherited from the orientation of
\Sigma^{3}
.

Definition 4.1
.

Denote by
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\coprod_{l\leq k}\Delta_{l}^{\epsilon_{l}}
a collection of ordered 2-simplices with orientation labelled by
\epsilon_{l}=\pm 1
. A
simplicial decomposition of
\Gamma^{2}
by
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
of legnth
k\geq 1
is the structure of a simplicial set on
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
— namely the data of face and degeneracy maps on the 2-simplices
\Delta_{l}
such that
e_{j}^{l_{j}}=\delta^{l}_{j}(\Delta_{j})
is the
l
-th face of the
j
-th 2-simplex
\Delta_{j}\in\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{2}
, with
1\leq j\leq k
and
1\leq l\leq 3,
— such that
\Gamma^{2}
is PL homeomorphic to the 2-truncated simplicial nerve

\Gamma^{2}\cong\big(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{2}\mathrel{\begin{subarray}{c}\textstyle\rightarrow\\[-2.58334pt]
\textstyle\rightarrow\\[-2.58334pt]
\textstyle\rightarrow\end{subarray}}\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{1}\rightrightarrows\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{0}\big).

Moreover, we say
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
is
regular
iff each edge is shared by at most by two distinct 2-simplices.

If
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
is regular, then we can write the PL identification as

\Gamma^{2}\cong\Delta_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}}\,{}_{e_{1}^{t_{1}}}\cup_{e_{2}^{s_{2}}}\Delta_{2}^{\epsilon_{2}}\,{}_{e_{2}^{t_{2}}}\cup_{e_{3}^{s_{3}}}\dots\,_{e_{k-1}^{t_{k-1}}}\cup_{e_{k}^{s_{k}}}\Delta_{k}^{\epsilon_{k}}.

The length
k
is simply the number of distinctly-labelled 2-simplices.

Here, the "incoming"
e^{t}\subset\partial\Delta
and "outgoing"
e^{\prime s}\subset\partial\Delta^{\prime}
edges of two oriented simplices
\Delta^{\epsilon},\Delta^{\prime\epsilon^{\prime}}\in\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{2}
are glued along a given PL homeomorhism
e^{t}\cong e^{\prime s}
, which can be either orientation preserving (
\epsilon=\epsilon^{\prime}
) or reversing (
\epsilon=-\epsilon^{\prime}
). Since only relative orientation matters in the gluing, we can always assume the orientation of
\Gamma^{2}
agrees with the first simplex
\Delta_{1}
, ie.
\epsilon_{1}=1
.

Definition 4.2
.

We call a vertex
v_{j}
in
\Delta_{j}
the
root vertex
if
v_{j}
is the source vertex of the distinguished source edge
e_{1}^{j}
of
\Delta_{j}
. We take as base point of
\Gamma
to be the root vertex
v=v_{1}
of
\Delta_{1}
.

Recall in the definition of the fundamental 2-simplex
\Delta
that the data of (i) a distinguished source edge, and (ii) its orientation determine the orientation of
\Delta
itself.

Definition 4.3
.

We say that the simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
of
\Gamma^{2}
with length
k
is
unbroken
if the distinguished source edges of
\Delta_{j}
,
1\leq j\leq k
, glue into a continuous PL path
p=p_{k}
in
\Gamma^{2}.
See fig.
3
.

Note we can always change the designated source edge label such that
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
is unbroken. The fact that the path
p
intersects the root vertices of every 2-simplex
\Delta\in\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{2}
is a key property which will be used later on.

Figure 3:
A typical complex of 2-simplices with different choice of source paths, coloured in red. The left is a unbroken configuration, and the right is broken.

It is clear that, if
p
is an oriented PL path, then its orientation determines uniquely a set of orientation data
\{\epsilon_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
for
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Suppose the simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
with length
k
is regular, then there exists an assignment of source edges to
\{\Delta_{j}\}_{j}
such that it is unbroken, with the length of
p
bounded by
|p|\leq k-1
.

Proof.

Recall how the source edges are defined: it is the "first" edge in a fundamental 2-simplex
\Delta
, and the rest of the edges are labelled in cyclic order according to the orientation of
\Delta
.

Prior to assuming regularity, we are going to record the indices
(t_{j};s_{l})
which label the edges appearing in the gluing data of the simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
.

Definition 4.4
.

Define the set
{\cal G}=\{(t_{j};s_{l})\}_{j,l}
of indices, where
j,l
runs over the indices for which we have a prescribed PL identification
e_{j}^{t_{j}}\cong e_{l}^{s_{l}}
of the corresponding gluing edges.

The condition of regularity then means that each edge in
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{1}
cannot have more than one gluing data: if
t_{j}=t_{j^{\prime}}
then
(t_{j};s_{l})=(t_{j^{\prime}};s_{l^{\prime}})
must coincide in
{\cal G}
. This then allows us to take
{\cal G}
as a subset of
(\mathbb{Z}_{3})^{k-1}
.

\boldsymbol{\Delta}
can in turn be made unbroken provided
t_{j}\neq s_{l}
if one of
t_{j},s_{l}
is not 1 — namely, we have to remove from
(\mathbb{Z}_{3})^{k-1}
the diagonal of the subset
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{3}
. This guarantees the existence of a PL continuous path
p
in
\Gamma
. We now partition
{\cal G}
into two subsets: one
{\cal G}_{2}
consisting of members of the form
(1;1)
and one
{\cal G}_{0.1}={\cal G}\setminus{\cal G}_{2}
that does not; it is from
{\cal G}_{0,1}
that we have to remove the diagonal.

These subsets have the following geometric meaning,

1.

{\cal G}_{0,1}
contains indices for the gluing edges
e_{j}^{t_{j}},e_{l}^{s_{l}}
for which at most only one of them is a source edge, and

2.

{\cal G}_{2}
contains those for which
both
of them are source edges.

It is then easy to see that gluing two 2-simplices along edges labelled in
{\cal G}_{0,1}
will increase the length of
p
by 1, while gluing along those in
{\cal G}_{2}
will increase
|p|
by 0. The length
p
is therefore bounded by the size of
{\cal G}_{0,1}
, which is
k-1
.
∎

Note a length
|p|=0
of zero is only possible in a regular simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
of length at most 2. The above proposition can be strengthened to ensure that the path
p
of length
k-1
is oriented, by including the data
\epsilon_{j}/\epsilon_{l}=\pm 1
of the relative orientations into the set
{\cal G}
.

In the following, we will always assume that
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
is equipped with a specification of source edges such that it defines a regular and unbroken simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
of
\Gamma
. Further, we shall also assume that the orientation data for the fundamental 2-simplices in
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
are determined uniquely (up to global orientation reversal) by the PL orientation of the path
p
.

Whiskering.

Fix a base point vertex
v\in\Gamma^{2}
. We denote by
p_{j}\subset\coprod_{l}\partial\Delta^{2}_{l}
some simplex path which connects
v
to the root vertex of
\Delta_{j}^{2}
, for all
1\leq j\leq k
. For a decorated 2-simplex
\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{j}}
, let
\phi_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{j}})
.

Definition 4.5
.

Define the
whiskering
of
\phi_{j}
to the base vertex
v\in\Gamma^{2}
as the meassureable field
W_{p_{j}}\phi
with stalk Hilbert spaces

(W_{p_{j}}\phi_{j})_{\mathrm{z}}=(\phi_{j})_{h_{p_{j}}\rhd\mathrm{z}},\qquad p_{j}=1\implies W_{p_{j}}\rhd-=\operatorname{id}.

From the perspective of sheaves,
W_{p_{j}}:\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{j}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{p_{j}\ast\Delta_{j}})
is the invertible direct image functor along the whiskering automorphism
h_{p_{j}}\rhd-:\mathbb{G}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}
, where
p_{j}\ast\Delta_{j}
is the attachment of the path
p_{j}
to the root vertex of
\Delta_{j}
.

Note a whiskering by the edge holonomy
h_{e}
cannot in general be removed through a 2-gauge transformation! Unless, of course,
h_{e}=a_{v}^{-1}a_{v^{\prime}}
is a pure gauge.

Note we can whisker along any path, not just the ones overlaying the distinguished source path on
\Gamma^{2}
obtained from
Proposition
4.1
.

Homotopies between whiskerings.

Consider two generic paths
p,p^{\prime}
which are homotopic in
\Gamma^{2}
. Let
D:p\Rightarrow p^{\prime}
denote the contractible closed face
D
spanned by them, which encloses several glued simplices. Due to fake-flatness
h_{p^{\prime}}=h_{p}\mathsf{t}(b_{D})
, the whiskering along
p
vs. that along
p^{\prime}
differ by a vertical multiplication of the face holonomy
b_{D}\in\mathsf{H}
.

This induces the translation operator
T_{D}:\xi_{\mathrm{z}}\mapsto\xi_{b_{D}\circ\mathrm{z}}
on sections of 2-graph states
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
. More precisely, we achieve the invertible bounded linear operators

T_{D}^{\phi}:W_{p}(\phi_{j})\rightarrow W_{p^{\prime}}(\phi_{j}),\qquad\forall\penalty\ \phi_{j}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{j}})

witnessing the difference between the whiskerings along
p,p^{\prime}
, where
\Delta_{j}
is the 2-simplex whose root vertex
v_{j}=p(1)=p^{\prime}(1)
is the endpoint of
p,p^{\prime}
. Imposing naturality against measureable morphisms, ie. the commutativity

T_{D}^{\phi^{\prime}}\circ W_{p}(f)=W_{p}(f)\circ T_{D},\qquad\forall\penalty\ f:\phi_{j}\to\phi_{j}^{\prime},

we can lift the above to the following.

Proposition 4.2
.

Each PL homotopy
D:p\Rightarrow p^{\prime}
between oriented paths
p,p^{\prime}
on
\Gamma^{2}
are witnessed by monoidal invertible measureable natural transformations
T_{D}:W_{p}\Rightarrow W_{p^{\prime}}
between the associated whiskering measureable functors.

The monoidality follows from the fact that the whiskering operation is monoidal,

W_{p}(\phi\otimes\phi^{\prime})\cong W_{p}\phi\otimes W_{p}\phi^{\prime},\qquad\phi,\phi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta})

where
\Delta
is the 2-simplex whose root vertex is the endpoint of the path
p
.

As such, provided
\Gamma^{2}
is unbroken and simply-connected, and that
p
starts at the root of
\Gamma^{2}
, then there is an invertible measureable natural transformation
W_{p}\Rightarrow W_{p_{j}}
which brings the whiskering by
p
to the whiskering by the source path
p_{j}
.

4.2
Dense states of 2-holonomies and 2-monodromies

We are finally ready to describe the construction of 2-simplex holonomies. We shall do this iteratively, starting from the case where the regular simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
has
k=2
. Let
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}\in\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{2}
be the 2-simplices in a regular simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
of
\Gamma
with the prescribed PL identification
f_{\epsilon}:e_{1}^{t}\xrightarrow{\sim}e_{2}^{s}
. Recall
\epsilon=\pm 1
keeps track of the orientation.

We now make use of the degeneracy maps
d_{j}^{l}
in the simplicial set
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
; denote by
d_{j}(e^{l}_{j})
the degenerate 2-simplex which collapse down to the
l
-th edge
e^{l}_{j}
of the
j
-th 2-simplex. We call
u_{12}=d_{1}(f_{\epsilon}(e_{1}^{t}))\cap d_{2}(e_{2}^{s})
the
(1,2)
-degeneracy intersection
. This subgraph has the property that its decorations have non-zero measure

\mu_{\Delta_{1}\coprod\Delta_{2}}(\mathbb{G}^{u_{12}})\neq 0

with respect to the Haar measure
\mu_{\Delta_{1}\coprod\Delta_{2}}
on the disjoint union decorated 2-simplices
\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{2}}=\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}\coprod\Delta_{2}}
.

By the classic Tietze extension theorem
[
156
,
157
]
, we can pick a smoothly interpolating function on
\mathbb{G}^{u_{12}}
to extend sections of
\phi_{1}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}})
, say, into the degeneracy intersection
u_{12}
.

Definition 4.6
.

The 2-graph state
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{2}})
is called
gluing-amenable
iff there exist an isomorphism of sheaves

\alpha_{12}:\phi_{1}\mid_{\mathbb{G}^{u_{12}}}\penalty\ \cong\penalty\ \phi_{2}\mid_{\mathbb{G}^{u_{12}}},\qquad\alpha_{12}=\alpha_{21}^{-1}.

We denote the gluing-amenable 2-graph states by
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}})\times_{e}\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{2}})
, where
e
is the gluing edge.

In essence, this condition allows us to "concatenate"
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
along the glued edges
f_{\epsilon}:e_{1}^{t}\xrightarrow{\sim}e_{2}^{s}
.

What this definition means more explicitly is the following. Let
\Gamma_{c}(H_{j}^{X_{12}})[[\hbar]]
denote the measureable sheaf of Hermitian sections corresponding to the restricted 2-graph states
\phi_{j}\mid_{X_{12}}
, where
j=1,2
and
X_{12}=\mathbb{G}^{u_{12}}
. The gluing-amenability condition is then the existence of an isomorphism
\Gamma_{c}(H_{1}^{X_{12}})[[\hbar]]_{/V}\cong\Gamma_{c}(H_{2}^{X_{12}})[[\hbar]_{/V}
of free
C(U)[[\hbar]]
-modules for each such Borel open
V\subset X_{12}
.

Let
p=p_{2}
denote the PL path from
v
to the root of
\Delta_{2}
, we then use the quantum deformed monoidal structure §
3.2.1
to define the
2-holonomy state

{\Phi}=\phi_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(h_{p_{2}}\rhd\phi_{2}),\qquad\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\penalty\ \text{ gluing-amenable}

associated to
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
.

We now wish to extend the notion of gluing-amenability to a regular simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
of
\Gamma
containing
k>2
number of fundamental 2-simplices. In order to do so, we first have to spell out the necessary coherence structure.

4.2.1
Interchangers; vertices of trisecitons

In §
4.2
, we have described how we can build 2-graphs
\Gamma
and 2-graph states on them from local data on each 2-simplex within it. We pause here to introduce a special geometric configuration of particular importance.

Let
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{4}
denote four oriented fundamental 2-simplices, which glues into the graph
\Gamma_{+}
specified by the following gluing configurations:

\Delta_{2i-1}^{+}\,{}_{e_{2i-1}^{2}}\cup_{e_{2i}^{3}}\Delta_{2i}^{+},\qquad\Delta_{i}^{+}\,{}_{e_{i}^{1}}\cup_{e_{i+2}^{1}}\Delta_{i+2}^{-},

where
i=1,2
. In other words, the resulting graph
\Gamma_{+}
is obtained by gluing a pair of the 2-simplices horizontally, and then gluing them vertically. Here, we have chosen the source edges to be
e_{i}=e_{i}^{1}\cong-e_{i+2}^{1}
for
i=1,2
, which is completely internal in
\Gamma_{+}
. We denote by the other glued edges by
e_{i}^{\prime}=e_{2i-1}^{2}\cong e_{2i}^{3}
, and the corresponding degeneracy intersection by
u_{1234}=u_{12}\cap u_{34}\cap u_{13}\cap u_{24}
around the central vertex.

The fact that
\Gamma_{+}
is well-defined unambiguously means that the simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\{\Delta^{\epsilon_{i}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{4}
, when considered as a 2-groupoid equipped with the given face maps and composition structures, satisfies the
interchange law
. This manifests as the gluing-amenability of 2=graph states on
\Gamma_{+}
.

Definition 4.7
.

We denote by
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\coprod_{i=1}^{4}\Delta_{i}})^{+}\subset\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\coprod_{i=1}^{4}\Delta_{i}})
the subcategory of holonomy-dense 2-simplex states
\phi_{i}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{i}})
which are (i) pairwise gluing-amenable and (ii) has equipped a measureable natural transformation

\beta:(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\otimes(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\Rightarrow\big((-\otimes-)\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(-\otimes-)\big)(1\times\text{swap}\times 1),

called the
interchanger
, between the functors
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\coprod_{i=1}^{4}\Delta_{i}})^{+}\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{+}})
, where "swap" is the swap functor.

Explicitly, this means that for pairwise gluing-amenable tuple of 2-simplex states
\phi_{i}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{i}})
, the components of
\beta
are isomorphisms of sheaves

\beta_{12}^{34}:(\phi_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi_{2})\otimes(\phi_{3}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi_{4})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{3})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(\phi_{2}\otimes\phi_{4})

between the 2-graph states restricted to
\mathbb{G}^{u_{1234}}
, where
u_{1234}
is the degeneracy intersection around the central vertex of
\Gamma_{+}
. Given holonomy-density, these extend to the natural transformation
\beta
.

Remark 4.1
.

In the undeformed case,
\beta
can be constructed as the natural isomorphism between the direct image functors of sheaves induced by the two ways
(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{i}})^{\times 4}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma+}
in which the decorated 2-simplices can be glued into decorations on
\Gamma_{+}
; see fig.
4
. Hence,
Definition
4.7
is saying that each such trisection in a 2-graph is assigned a natural interchange isomorphism
\beta
.

\Diamond

Figure 4:
The left illustrates the geometric configuration of 2-simplices upon which the interchanger

vertex
in a trisected singular graph

This isomorphism
\beta
witnesses the equivalence between the two valid ways of constructing holonomy-dense 2-graph states in
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{+}})
; since the deformed products of 2-graph states are used in the construction, the data of the interchanger
\beta
will also depend on
q
. We say
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{+}})

holonomy-dense
if the above functors
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\coprod_{i=1}^{4}\Delta_{i}})^{+}\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{+}})
are equivalences.

As such, the decomposition given in
Proposition
4.3
for holonomy-dense 2-graph states
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
is in fact in general not unique
on-the-nose
. They are only uniquely-defined up to the interchanger natural isomorphisms
\beta_{\Gamma_{+}}
on each subgraph
\Gamma_{+}\subset\Gamma
of the form described above.

Remark 4.2
.

Another geometric interpretation of the subgraph
\Gamma_{+}
is the following. Consider a graph
\Gamma\subset\Sigma
embedded in a 3d manifold
\Sigma
, and two other dsjoint 2-cells
C,C^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
in general position. Suppose they intersect
\Gamma
transversally, then their intersections
\Gamma\cap C\cap C^{\prime}
forms a "cross", which is precsiely what the internal tree
E^{\prime}
of the glued edges in
\Gamma_{+}
look like. As such, the data
\beta
can also be interpreted as a witnesses for
triple intersections
of surfaces in
\Sigma
. The fact that higher-gauge theories in 4d can detect triple intersections was noted in
[
158
,
1
]
.

\Diamond

Recall from the proof of
Proposition
4.1
that the set
{\cal G}
keeps tracks of the edge gluing data in
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
.

Definition 4.8
.

Suppose
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
has length
k>2
. The tuple
(\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{k})\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{k}})
is

iff

1.

each pair
\phi_{j},\phi_{l}
is gluing-amenable over the
(j,l)
-degeneracy intersection

where

run over the indices of the set
{\cal G}
,

2.

for each subgraph
\Gamma_{+}\subset\Gamma
of the above form, every pairwise gluing-amenable quadruple
(\phi_{j_{1}},\dots,\phi_{j_{4}})\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\coprod_{i=1}\Delta_{j_{i}}})
localized on
\Gamma_{+}
is in fact an element of the subcategory
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\coprod_{i=1}^{4}\Delta_{j_{i}}})^{+}
.

Definition 4.9
.

\Phi=\phi_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(W_{p_{1}}\phi_{2})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\dots\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(W_{p_{k}}\phi_{k})\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}).

(4.1)

When
\Gamma^{2}
has no boundary, we call the associated sheaf

the
2-monodromy state
.

We will typically consider
\Gamma

.

Call a regular unbroken simplicial decomposition

Definition 4.10
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
denote a regular unbroken oriented simplicial decomposition of
\Gamma
. We say the 2-graph state
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
is
holonomy-dense
iff there exists a gluing-amenable tuple
(\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{k})\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{k}})
such that
\phi
is measureably naturally isomorphic to 2-holonomy states
\Phi
of the form (
4.1
).

We say
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
itself is
holonomy-dense
if it consists of only holonomy-dense 2-graph states.

We are then able to iteratively construct 2-graphs states
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
from the products of (gluing-amenable) states living on the fundamental 2-simplices
\Delta_{j}\in\boldsymbol{\Delta}.
This is another expression of locality in our theory.

Now more generally, by using the derivation property (
3.5
) and the left-covariance (
3.11
) to bring all 2-gauge actions to the right, holonomy-density allows us to write each element in the lattice 2-algebra
\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma}
as products of sheaves localized on
distinct
2-simplices
\Delta_{j}
. The more precise statement is this.

Proposition 4.3
.

Denote by
\phi_{j_{1}}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{j_{1}}}),\dots,\phi_{j_{k}}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{j_{k}}})
a
k
-tuple of (possibly disjoint/non-gluing-amenable) 2-graph states for which the pair
(\phi_{j_{a}},\phi_{j_{b}})
is required to be gluing amenable only when the indices
(j_{a};j_{b})
appear in the set
{\cal G}
. If
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
were holonomy-dense, then there is an isomorphism of smooth sheaves

\displaystyle\phi\bullet\zeta\xrightarrow{\sim}\big(\phi_{j_{1}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(W_{p_{j_{2}}}\phi_{j_{2}})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\dots\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(W_{p_{j_{k}}}\phi_{j_{k}})\big)\bullet\zeta

for some

and

.

Note from §
3.1.3
that for

states with no gluing-amenability constraint, they live on disjoint 2-simplices and their product
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}
reduces to the undeformed product
\otimes
.
