4.3
Invariance modulo boundary

Fix a regular unbroken oriented simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
of
\Gamma
. The above formulation of
\Phi
is a direct generalization formulas given for the Chern-Simons holonomies in
[
23
]
, and they have the following analogous property.

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
E^{1}=\{e^{t_{j}}_{j}\cong e^{s_{l}}_{l}\}_{(t_{j};s_{l})\in{\cal G}}\subset\Gamma^{1}
denote the rooted tree of internal 1-graphs of
\Gamma
, consisting of edges across which the 2-simplices
\Delta\in\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{2}
are glued upon. If
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
were holonomy-dense, then there is a measureable isomorphism
\Lambda_{\zeta}\phi\xrightarrow{\sim}\phi
for all
\zeta\in\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{E^{1}}
.

Proof.

Recall from §
3.1.2
, §
3.1.3
that the the geometry/locality of the 2-gauge parameters
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
are dictated by the coproducts
\tilde{\Delta}
. We shall use to this to describe how 2-gauge transformations act on gluing-amenable 2-graph states.

By definition, a 2-gauge transformation localized to the edges
e_{j}^{t_{j}}\in\partial\Delta_{j},e_{l}^{s_{l}}\in\partial\Delta_{l}
act as the measureable endofunctors

\Lambda_{\zeta_{j}}=\Lambda_{(a_{v_{j}},\gamma_{e_{j}^{t_{j}}})}:\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{j}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{j}}),\qquad\Lambda_{\zeta_{l}}=\Lambda_{(a_{v_{l}},\gamma_{e_{l}^{s_{l}}})}:\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{l}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{l}}).

Suppose now we specify the gluing data, namely a PL identification
f_{\epsilon}:e_{j}^{t_{j}}\cong e_{l}^{s_{l}}
across which
\phi_{j},\phi_{l}
were gluing-amenable. The derivation property (
3.5
) then supplies a module tensorator
\varpi
(
3.3
) such that

\big(\varpi_{\zeta}^{W_{p_{j}}\phi_{j},W_{p_{l}}\phi_{l}}\big)^{-1}:\Lambda_{\zeta_{j}}(W_{p_{j}}\phi_{j})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Lambda_{\zeta_{l}}(W_{p_{l}}\phi_{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\big((\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{\Delta}(\zeta)}(W_{p_{j}}\phi_{j}\times W_{p_{l}}\phi_{l})\big),

as an invertible measureable natural transformation in
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{p_{j}\ast\Delta_{j}\coprod p_{l}\ast\Delta_{l}})
.

By definition of the coproduct
\tilde{\Delta}
, the 2-gauge parameter
\zeta=\zeta_{f_{\epsilon}(v_{j},e_{j}^{t_{j}})}\cdot\zeta_{(v_{l},e_{l}^{s_{l}})}
is obtained by horizontally stacking the 2-gauge transformations. However, given the path
p
is endowed with a framing which agrees with
\Delta_{j}
, then the 2-simplex
\Delta_{l}
interfacing with it must have the opposite framing. This framing reversal thus comes, according to (
3.55
), with an antipode
\tilde{S}
on
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
,

\zeta=(\tilde{S}\zeta)_{(v_{j},e_{j}^{t_{j}})}\times\zeta_{(v_{l},e_{l}^{s_{l}})};

see
Remark
3.11
.

Given the counit
\tilde{\epsilon}
and the unit
\tilde{\eta}=(1_{v},({\bf 1}_{1})_{e})
in
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
such that

\Lambda_{\tilde{\epsilon}(\zeta)}=\operatorname{id}_{\zeta},\qquad\Lambda_{\tilde{\eta}}=1_{\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})},

the Hopf axioms

(\tilde{S}\otimes 1)\tilde{\Delta}=(1\otimes\tilde{S})\tilde{\Delta}=\tilde{\epsilon}\otimes\tilde{\eta}

then provides an invertible natural transformation

(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\big((\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{\Delta}(\zeta)}(W_{p_{j}}\phi_{j}\times W_{p_{l}}\phi_{l})\big)\cong\Lambda_{\tilde{\epsilon}(\zeta)\cdot\tilde{\eta}}(W_{p_{j}}\phi_{j}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}W_{p_{l}}\phi_{l})\cong W_{p_{j}}\phi_{j}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}W_{p_{l}}\phi_{l}.

Due to the locality of the edges in
E^{1}
, we can repeat the above argument for each edge in
E^{1}
such that we achieve an invertible measureable natural transformation
\varphi_{\zeta}
on the 2-holonomy states,

\varphi_{\zeta}^{\Phi}:\Lambda_{\zeta}\Phi\xrightarrow{\sim}\Phi,\qquad\forall\penalty\ \Phi.

The holonomy-density of
\phi
then allows us to extend this to
\varphi_{\zeta}^{\phi}:\Lambda_{\zeta}\phi\xrightarrow{\sim}\phi
in
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
.
∎

Note this isomorphism is natural against measureable morphisms between holonomy-dense measureable fields: there is a sheaf automorphism
f\circ\varphi^{\phi}_{\zeta}=\varphi_{\zeta}^{\phi^{\prime}}\circ f
for all
f:\phi\rightarrow\phi^{\prime}
.

An immediate corollary is therefore the following.

Corollary 4.5
.

If
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
were holonomy-dense, then it is a homotopy fixed point under
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{E^{1}}
. Therefore, holonomy-dense
2-monodromy states are observable
:

\partial\Gamma=\emptyset\implies\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\subset\mathcal{O}^{\Gamma}.

Proof.

If
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
were holonomy-dense, then the natural isomorphisms
\varphi_{\zeta}^{\phi}
organize into an invertible measureable natural transformation
\varphi_{\zeta}:\Lambda_{\zeta}\Rightarrow 1_{\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})}
. The corollary then follows from the triangle axioms

\varphi_{\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime}}=\varphi_{\zeta}\ast(\Lambda_{\zeta}\circ\varphi_{\zeta^{\prime}})\ast\alpha^{\Lambda}_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}

satisfied by
\varphi
against the
\Lambda
-module associator
\alpha^{\Lambda}_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}:\Lambda_{\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime}}(-)\Rightarrow\Lambda_{\zeta}\circ(\Lambda_{\zeta^{\prime}}(-))
.
11
11

11

Note
\alpha^{\Lambda}
can be obtained through (
3.11
) from the
\bullet
-module associator
\alpha^{\bullet}
given in the definition
\mathcal{B}^{\Gamma}=\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\rtimes\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
as a monoidal semidierct product.

The second statement follows directly from the definition: if
\partial\Gamma=\emptyset
, then any holonomy-dense 2-graph state
\Phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
satisfies the invariance condition (
3.53
).
∎

Note if
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}
are localized on disjoint edges in
E^{1}
, then
\Lambda_{\zeta},\Lambda_{\zeta^{\prime}}
commute up to a natural measureable isomorphism by locality (see §
3.1.3
).

4.4
Disjoint commutativity modulo boundary

We now turn to general simplicial decompositions of a 2-graph, in which each edge is not shared by necessarily at most two faces in
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
. To build such a structure up from the regular one, we first set up the local geometry, where a 2-simplex intersects a graph
\Gamma
at one of its
internal
edges.

Provided
\Gamma
itself has equipped a regular (unbroken oriented) simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
, there is then a 2-subgraph
\Gamma_{e}
local to an internal edge
e\in E^{1}
, satisfying the property that its induced regular simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{e}\subset\boldsymbol{\Delta}
has size
k=2
.

We fix the labels
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}\in\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{e}
and the associated gluing data on
e
as a PL identification
e=e_{1}^{t_{1}}\xrightarrow{\sim}e_{2}^{s_{2}}
. For simplicity, we shall pick the base point of
\Gamma_{e}
to be contained within the glued edge. This is such that no whiskering needs to be performed when forming local holonomy-dense 2-graph states on
\Gamma_{e}
.

Now suppose a third fundamental simplex
\Delta^{\prime}
intersects
\Gamma_{e}
at its internal gluing edge
e
, whence this edge is shared by
three
simplices. We denote the resulting graph by
\Gamma^{\prime}_{e}
, which is equipped with a non-regular simplicial decomposition.

4.4.1
Non-regular 2-graphs; triple points

Prior to studying properties of the holonomy-dense 2-graph states on
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{\prime}_{e}})
, however, we must promote our notion of "gluing-amenability" to non-regular simplicial decompositions.

Suppose three fundamental 2-simplices
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}
are incident upon the same edge
e
. Denote by
u_{123}=u_{12}\cap u_{13}\cap u_{13}
the triple intersection of the pairwise degeneracy intersections
u_{12},u_{13},u_{23}
, and we label the pairwise sheaf automorphisms

\alpha_{ij}:\phi_{i}\mid_{\mathbb{G}^{u_{ij}}}\cong\phi_{j}\mid_{\mathbb{G}^{u_{jk}}},\qquad 1\leq i<j\leq 3

as provided in
Definition
4.6
.

Definition 4.11
.

We say the triple
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})_{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}\coprod\Delta_{2}\coprod\Delta_{3}})
is
gluing-amenable
iff there is a
U(1)
-phase
\sigma_{123}\in U(1)
, localized on 2-holonomy decorations on the triple intersection
u_{123}
, such that the associated sheaf isomorphisms
\alpha_{ij}
satisfies

\alpha_{23}\circ\alpha_{12}=\sigma_{123}\cdot\alpha_{13}.

Moreover, if
\Delta_{4}
is another 2-simplex incident upon this same edge
e
, then on the quadruple intersection
u_{1234}
this phase satisfies the pentagon condition

(\delta\sigma)_{1234}=\sigma_{234}\sigma_{134}^{-1}\sigma_{124}\sigma_{123}^{-1}=1,

which ensures that the assignment of
U(1)
-phases
\sigma
is well-defined

The above pentagon condition bears a striking resemblance to Čech 2-cocycle conditions, hence we shall denote by
\check{H}(\mathbb{G}^{u_{123}},U(1))
the space in which such
U(1)
-phases
\sigma
live. As a slight abuse of language, we shall refer to
\sigma
as the
U(1)
-gerbe
attached to a triple degeneracy intersection
u_{123}
.

Remark 4.4
.

In the undeformed case,
\sigma
can be constructed as the natural isomorphism between the pushforward functors of sheaves induced by the composites

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}\cup_{e}\Delta_{2}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{3}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{2}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{3}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{e}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{2}\cup_{e}\Delta_{3}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

on the decorated 2-simplices; see fig.
5
. As such,
Definition
4.7
is saying that each such triple point in a 2-graph is assigned a natural isomorphism
\sigma
, and due to naturality of the
\alpha
’s this isomorphism can be reduced to be
U(1)
-valued on each stalk over
\mathbb{G}^{u_{123}}
. In the weak case, the Postnikov anomaly/defect
\tau
contributes
directly
to
\sigma
.

\Diamond

Figure 5:
The left illustrates the geometric configuration of 2-simplices upon which the
U(1)
-gerbe
\sigma
is defined. This geometry is precisely the
triple point
in a singular graph
[
75
]
as displayed on the right; see also fig. 2 (b) of
[
76
]
.

The data
\sigma
will be implicit in the following.

Theorem 4.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
be holonomy-dense, and let
\Delta^{\prime}
be another fundamental 2-simplex which intersects
\Gamma
at one of its internal edges
e\in E^{1}
. Then provided
(\Phi_{e},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{e}\coprod\Delta^{\prime}})
is gluing-amenable, there exists measureable isomorphisms of sheaves

\phi^{\prime}\times\Phi_{e}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Phi_{e}\times\phi^{\prime},\qquad\Phi_{e}\times\phi^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}\phi^{\prime}\times\Phi_{e}

in
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{e}\coprod\Delta^{\prime}})\cong\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{e}})\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

If
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
is holonomy-dense, so are the 2-graph states on any subgraph of
\Gamma
. Thus in particular
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{e}})
is holonomy-dense, hence there exists a gluing-amenable tuple
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Delta_{1}\coprod\Delta_{2}})
(near the
(1,2)
-degeneracy intersection
u_{12}
around the edge
e\in E^{1}
) such that sections in
\Phi_{e}
are equivalent to sections in
\phi_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{e}})
on the subgraph
\Gamma_{e}
. Note no whiskering needs to be done on
\Gamma_{e}
as we have assumed that the base point
v
of
\Gamma_{e}
is contained in
e
.

Given this setup, we then have a dense inclusion of sheaves of sections

(1\times-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)(\phi^{\prime}\times\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2})=\phi^{\prime}\times(\phi_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi_{2})\subset\phi^{\prime}\times\Phi_{e}.

By hypothesis,
\partial\Delta^{\prime}\cap e\neq\emptyset
. If we pick the local framing of the interface
e
to coincide with the framings of
\Delta_{1}
, then we have a measureable isomorphism of sheaves

\phi^{\prime}\times\phi_{1}\cong(\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{R}_{e}}(\phi_{1}\times\phi^{\prime})

by the
braid relations
(
3.12
), where
\tilde{R}_{e}
is the 2-
R
-matrix on
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{e}
. On the other hand, once we have fixed the framing of
e
as above, it must be opposite to that of
\Delta_{2}
. Hence (
3.55
)

\phi^{\prime}\times\phi_{2}\cong(\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{(1\times\tilde{S})\tilde{R}_{e}}(\phi_{2}\times\phi^{\prime});

see
Remark
3.11
.

We now combine these two computations through the gluing-amenability condition
Definition
4.11
. Using the module associator

(\alpha^{\Lambda\times\Lambda}_{\tilde{R},(1\times\tilde{S})\tilde{R}}):(\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{R}}\circ(\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{(1\times\tilde{S})\tilde{R}}\Rightarrow(\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{R}\penalty\ \hat{\cdot}\penalty\ (1\times\tilde{S})\tilde{R}},

together with one of the quasitriangularity axioms satisfied by the cobraiding
\tilde{R}
,
12
12

12

Here
\hat{\cdot}
denotes the contraction

A\penalty\ \hat{\cdot}\penalty\ B=(1\times-\cdot-\times 1)(A\times B),\qquad A,B\in\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}\times\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}.

\tilde{R}\penalty\ \hat{\cdot}\penalty\ (1\times\tilde{S})\tilde{R}=\tilde{\eta}\times\tilde{\eta},

we finally achieve a measureable isomorphism of sheaves

\phi^{\prime}\times(\phi_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi_{2})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\phi_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi_{2})\times\phi^{\prime}.

This extends to
\Phi_{e}
by density arguments.

Similar argument applies to produce a sheaf isomorphism

(\phi_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi_{2})\times\phi^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}\phi^{\prime}\times(\phi_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi_{2})

from the other quasitriangularity axiom

(\tilde{S}\times 1)\tilde{R}\penalty\ \hat{\cdot}\penalty\ \tilde{R}=\tilde{\eta}\times\tilde{\eta}.

∎

Keep in mind that, in general, the above sheaf isomorphisms need
not
be inverses of each other.

4.4.2
Consistency with the interchanger

We now wish to extend the above argument to
any
regular graph
\Gamma^{\prime}
which meets the given
\Gamma
at a collection of internal edges of
\Gamma
in
E^{1}
. To do this, however, we need to understand how the
U(1)
-gerbes
\sigma
"stack" against each other. This involves the planar interchanger
\beta
.

The geometric setup is the following. Let
\Gamma_{e},\Gamma_{e^{\prime}}
denote graphs of the form above: each consisting of three fundamental 2-simplices glued at the same edges
e,e^{\prime}
, respectively. Given then edges
e,e^{\prime}
are composable

\exists v_{\circ},\qquad e\cup_{v_{\circ}}e^{\prime}=v\xrightarrow{e}v_{\circ}\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}}v^{\prime},

we can introduce additional gluing data which stacks these graphs together along (all) their source edges:
e_{i}^{1}\xrightarrow{\sim}e_{i}^{\prime 1}
(see also
Remark
4.5
). We denote the resulting graph by
\Gamma=\Gamma_{e\cup_{v_{\circ}}e^{\prime}}
.

Remark 4.5
.

This graph
\Gamma=\Gamma_{e\cup_{v_{\circ}}e^{\prime}}=\Gamma_{e}\cup_{\coprod_{i=1}^{3}e_{i}^{1}}\Gamma_{e}^{\prime}
is obtained by gluing
all
three of the source edges along
e_{i}^{1}\cong e_{i}^{\prime 1}
, for all
i=1,2,3
. If we only glue one of the source edges, say
e_{1}^{1}\cong e_{1}^{\prime 1}
, then we produce a different geometry
\Gamma_{e\cup_{1}e^{\prime}}
than what was described above (see the upper row of fig.
6
). Further, if we glue two of the source edges, then we can introduce another triple point graph
\Gamma_{e^{\prime\prime}}
forming a trivalent vertex
v_{\circ}
with the edges
e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}
(see the lower row of fig.
6
). This configuration witnesses the associativity

(\sigma\cup_{2}\sigma^{\prime})\cup_{2}\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\sigma\cup_{2}(\sigma^{\prime}\cup_{2}\sigma^{\prime\prime})

of the associated
U(1)
-gerbes
\sigma^{(^{\prime}),(^{\prime\prime})}\in\check{H}^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{u_{123}^{(^{\prime}),(^{\prime\prime})}},U(1))
under the gluing product
\cup_{2}
.

\Diamond

Figure 6:
The geometric configurations involving the different gluing operations between non-regular triple point 2-graphs
\Gamma_{e},\Gamma_{e^{\prime}}
. The upper row displays their gluing along a single source edge

The degeneracy neighborhood around the central vertex

along the vertical composition operation
\otimes

by

.

On the other hand, for

glued along their source edges

for

(\alpha_{23}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\alpha_{2^{\prime}3^{\prime}})\circ(\alpha_{12}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\alpha_{1^{\prime}2^{\prime}})=\sigma_{(11^{\prime})(22^{\prime})(33^{\prime})}\cdot(\alpha_{13}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\alpha_{2^{\prime}3^{\prime}})

near the central vertex
v_{\circ}.
This also defines a
U(1)
-gerbe, which we denote by
\sigma\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\in\check{H}^{2}(\mathbb{G}^{u},U(1))
.

The notion of "gluing-amenability" for generic non-regular simplicial decompositions therefore must involve consistency relations between the
U(1
)-gerbes
\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime},\sigma\cdot\sigma^{\prime}
living on subgraphs of the form
\Gamma

Let
u=u_{123}\cap u_{123}^{\prime}

of

. We now introduce the

-phases

(see

) which witness the commutativity of
\alpha
with the interchangers
\beta
,
13
13

13

Here we have abbreviated
\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{i,i+1}:\phi_{i}\mid_{u_{i,i+1}}\cong\phi_{i+1}\mid_{u_{i,i+1}}
for

, where

.

\displaystyle\beta_{23}^{2^{\prime}3^{\prime}}\circ\big((\alpha_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\alpha_{2})\otimes(\alpha_{1^{\prime}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\alpha_{2^{\prime}})\big)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{12}\cdot\big((\alpha_{1}\otimes\alpha_{1^{\prime}})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(\alpha_{2}\otimes\alpha_{2^{\prime}})\big)\circ\beta_{12}^{1^{\prime}2^{\prime}},

Geometrically, these
U(1)
-phases
\gamma
witness the compatibility of the configuration of simplices indicated in fig.
7
.

Figure 7:
The figure illustrates the geometric configuration of 2-simplices upon which
\gamma
witnesses the compatibility of the interchanger
\beta
with the sheaf isomorphisms
\alpha
.

The condition is then that these phases implements the consistency of the products
\cdot,\cup
,

\gamma_{12}\gamma_{13}^{-1}\gamma_{23}=(\sigma_{123}\otimes\sigma_{123}^{\prime})(\sigma_{(11^{\prime})(22^{\prime})(33^{\prime})})^{-1},

By translating this into the language of the Čech cocycle
\delta\eta
, we have the following.

Definition 4.12
.

We say that the tuple
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3};\phi_{1}^{\prime},\phi_{2}^{\prime},\phi_{3}^{\prime})_{\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\coprod_{i=1}^{3}(\Delta_{i}\times\Delta_{i}^{\prime})})
is
gluing-amenable
on
\Gamma
iff there exists a Čech 1-cocycle
\gamma\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{G}^{u},U(1))
such that

(\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime})=\delta\gamma(\sigma\cdot\sigma^{\prime}).

(4.26)

In other words, the two operations
\cdot,\cup
coincide in Čech cohomology on
\mathbb{G}^{u}
, where
u=u_{123}\cap u_{123}^{\prime}
.

This condition ensures that the
U(1)
-gerbe attached to states on graphs of the form
\Gamma=\Gamma_{e\cup_{v_{\circ}}e^{\prime}}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}\Gamma_{i}
is unambiguously
\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime}
.

Remark 4.6
.

Note crucially that (
4.26
)
requires
the sheaf automorphisms
\beta,\alpha
to commute only up to a phase. In general, these define sheaf automorphisms
\gamma
on quadruple tensor products of 2-graph states, hereby abbreviated as "
\phi^{4}
". However, since these sheaf isomorphisms
\alpha,\beta
are natural (ie. commute with all appropriate measureable morphisms of sheaves), this sheaf automorphism must commute with all bounded linear operators on
\phi^{4}
. This forces
\eta\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
to be a scalar, which can be normalized to a
U(1)
-phase.

\Diamond

Example 4.1
.

Let
P\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
denote the union of the three coordinate planes in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and consider a 2-graph
\Gamma^{2}
which triangulates
P\cap D^{3}
, where
D^{3}
is the unit 3-disc. This geometric configuration consists of the stacking of two subgraphs of the form
\Gamma_{e}^{\prime}\cup_{e}\Delta_{4}
, where
\Gamma_{e}^{\prime}
is the graph around a triple point as described in
Remark
4.4
. In accordance with
Definition
4.12
, gluing-amenable 2-graph states on each wedge in
\Gamma=\Gamma^{2}
has attached a
U(1)
-valued Čech 2-cocycle of the form
\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime}
. The difference between these gerbes across the wedges are described by precisely
the Leibniz rule
,

\delta(\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime})=\delta\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime}+\sigma\cup\delta\sigma^{\prime},

whence the 2-cocycle condition in
Definition
4.11
says that the
U(1)
-gerbe attached to
\Gamma
is unambiguously given by the Čech cohomology class of
\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime}
.

4.4.3
Braiding properties of the 2-graph operator products

Recall that, for each 2-graph
\Gamma
, the 2-graph states are modelled as a measureable category of
L^{2}\otimes\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]
-sheaves over
X=(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}},\mu_{\Gamma^{2}})
in
Definition
3.11
.

We now examine how each such measureable category behave depending on the locality of the 2-graphs.

Theorem 4.7
.

For each 2-graph
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
, define the functor

c:\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}),\qquad\Phi\times\Phi^{\prime}\mapsto(\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{R}}\Phi^{\prime}\times\Phi.

(4.27)

If
\Gamma^{1}\cap\partial\Gamma^{\prime}
contains at most 0-simplices, then there exists a trivialization
c\cong\operatorname{id}
.

Proof.

Note the functor
c
, as defined, depends on where
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
is localized — namely how the 1-graph
\Gamma^{1}\hookrightarrow\Sigma
is embedded into the 3d PL Cauchy surface in relation to the graphs
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
. By
Corollary
4.5
, 2-gauge transformations
\Lambda
act non-trivially only on the boundary, hence we can without loss of generality assume
\Gamma^{1}\subset\partial\Gamma
is localized to the boundary of, say, the graph
\Gamma
.

1.

\Gamma^{1}\cap\partial\Gamma^{\prime}
contains only 0-simplices
:
\Gamma^{\prime}
ends on a set
E=\Gamma^{1}
of internal edges of
\Gamma
. We can then decompose
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\cup_{E}\Gamma_{2}
further, whence by holonomy-density, we can apply the argument in
Theorem
4.6
to each local graph intersection along
e\in E
. The condition
Definition
4.12
then allows us to extend this argument along composite internal edges
e\cup_{v_{\circ}}e^{\prime}
, and hence to the entire collection
E
. This gives a natural isomorphism
c_{\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}),\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})}\Rightarrow\text{flip}
which trivializes the braiding on the gluing-amenable states
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\times_{E}\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})
.

2.

\Gamma^{1}\cup\partial\Gamma^{\prime}=\emptyset
is empty
: in this case,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
are disjoint, whence
\tilde{R}
acts trivially by (
3.12
). The braiding functor
c

is
just the flip functor.

The final statement follows immediately from
Definition
4.9
.
∎

In other words, the extended operator insertions commute on 2-graphs with "decloaized boundaries". This is the categorical analogue of Thm. 1 in
[
71
]
: the closed plaquette elements
c^{I}(P)
are central in
{\cal A}_{CS}
.

Remark 4.7
.

By locality §
3.1.3
, the 2-graph states
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\in\operatorname{Mod}^{*}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G})
form a measureable *-module category over the categorical quantum enveloping algebra
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
for
any
2-graph
\Gamma
. Due to the higher-Yang-Baxter equations satisfied by the 2-
R
-matrix (cf.
[
144
,
1
]
), the functor
c
(
4.27
) induces a braided monoidal structure on
\operatorname{Mod}^{*}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G})

[
160
,
62
]
. If we further replace
\mathsf{Meas}
with
\mathsf{2Hilb}
, then we would recover the
ribbon tensor
2-category
\operatorname{2Rep}({\mathbb{U}}_{q}\mathfrak{G})
of 2-representations studied in
[
62
]
; see also
Remark
3.3
.

\Diamond

An immediate consequence of
Theorem
4.7
is that 2-monodromy states — namely the
closed
Wilson surface states — commute with all other 2-graph states. In the context of
Remark
4.7
, it means that the "closed-surface sector" of 2-Chern-Simons theory is contained within the
E_{2}
-centre

Z_{2}(\operatorname{Mod}^{*}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}))
; this will be made precise in the companion work. This fact is a concrete manifestation of the general idea that the closed-brane sector of higher-dimensional QFT lies, in an appropriate sense, in the centre of the open-brane sector
[
54
]
.

Remark 4.8
.

By definition of the 2-holonomies
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}
(
Definition
3.1
), open Wilson surface states can only be described by the theory of
non-Abelian gerbes
afforded by principal 2-bundles
[
18
,
161
,
80
,
117
]
. Indeed, 2-gauge theories with a trivial structure map
\mu_{1}=0
can only describe Wilson surface states that are closed
[
102
,
19
,
162
]
. As such,
any
higher-gauge TQFT built from representation theories of the types listed in
Remark
3.4
will not be able to explicitly describe open-brane sectors.

\Diamond

4.5
Orientation reversals and frame rotations

To close this section off, let us investigate the what the *-operations defined in §
3.3.3
imply through holonomy-density.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
be holonomy-dense, then there are meausreable natural isomorphisms

-^{*_{1}}\xRightarrow{\sim}(\Lambda\otimes 1)_{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{h}}^{-1}}}\circ-^{\dagger_{1}},\qquad-^{*_{2}}\xRightarrow{\sim}(\Lambda\otimes 1)_{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{v}}^{-1}}}\circ-^{\dagger_{2}}

whose underlying measureable morphism at each component
\phi
is given by the 2-
\dagger
-intertwining pair
\eta
. Here, each relevant
\tilde{R}
-matrices are localized on
\partial\Gamma
. If
\partial\Gamma
, then
\tilde{R}
is localized on the base point
v\in\Gamma
.

Proof.

By holonomy-density, this follows directly from
Definition
3.16
and
Definition
3.17
.
∎

What this means more explicitly is the following. If we write
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})^{*_{1}}
to denote the set of objects of
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
under the image
-^{*_{1}}
, for instance, then we have measureable isomorphisms

\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})^{*_{1}}\cong(\Lambda\otimes 1)_{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{h}}^{-1}}\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{\dagger_{1}}}),\qquad\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})^{*_{2}}\cong(\Lambda\otimes 1)_{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{v}}^{-1}}\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{\dagger_{2}}})

coming from the 2-
\dagger
-intertwiners
\eta=(\eta_{\mathrm{h}},\eta_{\mathrm{v}})
,
as well as
the sheaf isomorphism induced by the module associator
\alpha^{\Lambda\otimes 1}_{\tilde{R},\tilde{R}^{-1}}:(\Lambda\otimes 1)_{\tilde{R}}\circ(\Lambda\otimes 1)_{\tilde{R}^{-1}}\cong(\Lambda\otimes 1)_{{\tilde{R}\penalty\ \hat{\cdot}\tilde{R}^{-1}}}\cong 1_{\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})}
.

Further, the natural isomorphisms defined above commutes with those coming from the strong-commutativity
(-^{*_{1}})^{\text{op}}\circ-^{*_{2}}\cong(-^{*_{2}})^{\text{m-op,c-op}}\circ-^{*_{1}}
of the *-operations.

Definition 4.13
.

The
flatness of the 2-holonomies
is the notion that, if
V
is a contractible 3-cell, then
\prod_{f\in\partial V}b_{f}=1
for all
\mathrm{z}=\{(h_{e},b_{f})\}_{(e,f)}\in\mathbb{G}^{\partial V}
. As such, if
V
is represented by a PL homotopy
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
then the 2-holonomies on
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
are 2-gauge equivalent.

This is well-known fact in
strict
higher-gauge theory
[
17
,
18
,
4
,
16
,
35
,
101
]
.

By "full-stacking", we mean a PL identification of two 2-simplices
everywhere
(ie. not just at one of their edges).

Remark 4.9
.

In weak 2-Chern-Simons theory, the Postnikov class of
\mathbb{G}

[
11
,
57
,
102
,
163
]
gives the anomaly/defect that breaks precisely the 2-flatness condition
[
59
,
18
,
20
]
:
\prod_{f\in\partial V}h_{v}=\tau_{h_{e_{1}},h_{e_{2}},h_{e_{3}}}
. This leads to non-trivial modifications between whiskering pseudonaturals as described in
Remark
6.3
, and also induce a
first descendant
modification between 2-gauge transformations (this was described in
[
1
]
). The presence of
\tau
necessitates the categorification step described in
Remark
1.1
, as the 2-cells/squares (ie. the
u
in (
3.1
)) allow room to keep track of the higher coherences that appear due to
\tau
.

\Diamond

We now leverage 2-flatness to prove a categorical, "basis-independent" analogue of Prop. 7 in
[
71
]
.

Proposition 4.9
.

Suppose
\Gamma=\Delta\cup_{\Delta}\bar{\Delta}
consist of the full-stacking of a fundamental 2-simplex
\Delta
with its orientation reversal
\bar{\Delta}=\Delta^{\dagger_{1}}
, then holonomy-dense 2-graph states on
\Gamma
is trivial:
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\simeq\mathsf{Hilb}
.

Proof.

The full-stacking of
\Delta
and its orientation reversal
\bar{\Delta}
gives rise to a
closed
2-graph
\Gamma^{2}
which comes equipped with a null-homotopy
\Gamma^{2}\simeq v
. Thus by 2-flatness
Definition
4.13
, the 2-holonomies on
\bar{\Delta},\Delta
are 2-gauge equivalent: for each fixed
\mathrm{z}\in\mathbb{G}^{\bar{\Delta}}
and
\mathrm{z}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{G}^{\Delta}
, we can find a 2-gauge transformation
\zeta\in\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
for which
\operatorname{hAd}^{-1}_{\zeta}\mathrm{z}=\mathrm{z}^{\prime}
— or, in other words,
\mathrm{z}^{-1_{h}}\cdot_{h}\mathrm{z}^{\prime}
is a
pure 2-gauge
.

Therefore, through holonomy-density and 2-
\dagger
unitarity §
3.3.2
, each 2-graph state
\Phi={\phi}\otimes\phi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
by 2-flatness is a pure 2-gauge state (namely one with support only on pure 2-gauge 2-holonomies). By construction, pure 2-gauge holonomy configurations can be removed by a 2-gauge transformation §
3.1.2
. But since
\Gamma^{2}
has no boundary,
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
only has 2-monodromy states, which are 2-gauge invariant up to homotopy by
Proposition
4.5
.

This means that there is a measureable isomorphism
\Phi\cong\eta
to the unit
\eta\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
, which removes
all
of the 2-holonomy decorations on any 2-graph state
\Phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
. The unit, by definition, can be viewed as a full measureable functor
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{v})\simeq\mathsf{Hilb}\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
from states on the trivial 2-graph
v
. The above argument then means that every 2-graph state in
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
lives in the essential image of this functor, giving us the desired equivalence

\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\simeq\mathsf{Hilb}.

∎

Gluing-amenability then allows us to extend
Proposition
4.9
to entire 2-graphs.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
\bar{\Gamma}=\Gamma^{\dagger_{1}}
denote the orientation reversed simplicial complex of
\Gamma
, then there is an equivalence

\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma\cup_{\Gamma}\bar{\Gamma}})\simeq\mathsf{Hilb}

on holonomy-dense 2-graph states on the full-stacking
\Gamma\cup_{\Gamma}\bar{\Gamma}
.

Proof.

By gluing-amenability, we can use the interchanger isomorphisms
\beta

Definition
4.7
to break 2-graph states on
\Gamma\cup_{\Gamma}\bar{\Gamma}
to a product of 2-graph states on the stacking
\Delta_{j}\cup_{\Delta_{j}}\bar{\Delta}_{j}
of each fundamental 2-simplex
\Delta_{j}
contained in
\Gamma
. The result then follows by applying
Proposition
4.9
repeatedly.
∎

The results of these sections, §
4.3
, §
4.4
and §
4.5
, are direct higher-dimensional generalizations of part (1), (2) and (3) of Proposition 2, 3 in
[
71
]
.
14
14

14

That is, except the first formula in part (3) of these propositions. This formula expands the tensor products of the quantum algebra
C_{q}(G^{\Gamma^{1}})
in a basis, resulting in the Clebsch-Goran coefficients. We had not done this here, as to do so for
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
we require a categorical Peter-Weyl theorem. We leave this to a companion work.
Though many subtleties arise in the weak case (cf.
Remark
2.2
), we expect lax versions of the results of these sections to continue to hold.

5
Categorified states: additive measureable *-functors

Recall that the usual notion of a
normalized state
on a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A
is a linear funciotnal
\psi:A\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
for which
\psi(1)=1

[
164
,
22
]
. The space of such linear functionals serves as the physical Hilbert space of states in the quantum theory.

The goal in this section is to introduce a categorified version of these states. The guiding principle is once again
\mathsf{Meas}
, the 2-category of measureable categories
[
26
]
. Indeed, there is a natural equivalence
\mathsf{Hilb}\simeq{\cal H}^{\emptyset}
with the measureable category over the empty set. Moreover, considering
\mathsf{Meas}
as a monoidal bicategory (see Thm. 50,
[
26
]
),
\mathsf{Hilb}
is the monoidal identity.

Global measureable change of basis.

Let
\{H_{x}\}_{x\in X}
be a family of Hilbert spaces over the measure space
(X,\mu)
and let
R
be a local ring over
\mathbb{C}
(such as when
R=C(Y),L^{2}(Y,\mu^{\prime})
for some other manifold/measure space
(Y,\mu^{\prime})
). The following proposition will be useful.

Proposition 5.1
.

If each
H_{x}
is a (finitely-generated projective)
R
-module, then the direct integral
\displaystyle\int_{X}^{\oplus}d\mu(x)H_{x}
is a (finitely-generated projective)
R
-module. Conversely, if
H
is a
R
-module and admits a direct integral decomposition
\displaystyle\int_{X}^{\oplus}d\mu(x)H_{x}
, then each
H_{x}
is also a
R
-module.

Proof.

If
v\sim_{\mu}v
are
\mu
-a.e. equivalent sections in
\coprod_{x\in X}H_{x}
, then
v-u\sim_{\mu}0
, hence
r\cdot u-r\cdot v=r\cdot(u-v)\sim_{\mu}0
and hence
r\cdot u\sim_{\mu}r\cdot u
are also
\mu
-a.e. equivalent sections for any
r\in R
. The converse is a special case of a theorem in the work of Segal
[
165
]
(see also Thm. 1.2 (iii) in
[
166
]
), where we simply replace the
W^{*}
-algebra
A\cong L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
with
A\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}R
.
∎

In other words, if
R
is "constant across
X
", then the direct integral will also inherit the
R
-module structure and vice versa.
15
15

15

The author believes that there should be a much more general version of the above statement where
R
is allowed to be local along
X
, provided the local
R_{x}
-module structure is allowed to vary in a
\mu
-essentially bounded manner across
x\in X
. We will not need such a powerful statement here, however.

5.1
Categorical linear *-functionals on 2-graph states

In accordance with the above setup, we will model such "categorical linear functionals" as an additive measureable functor of sheaves

\omega:\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\rightarrow\mathsf{Hilb},

where we are considering
\mathsf{Hilb}
as the category of sections of Hermitian vector bundles over the singleton
\ast
. Here, additive means that
\omega
respects the direct sum of sheaves, but it need
not
respect any monoidal structure!

In this section, we will prove a Yoneda embedding
Proposition
5.2
for
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\subset\mathsf{Meas}_{X}
by just treating it as a full subcategory of measureable fields of over
X=(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma},\mu_{\Gamma^{3}})
, as in
Definition
3.11
. We will come back to deal with the
internal
/double cocategory structure in §
6.1.3
.

5.1.1
Evaluation states; cone functors on
\Lambda\Gamma^{2}

We begin with a connected PL 2-manifold
S
equipped with an oriented simplicial decomposition
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
. The resulting graph
\Gamma
of
S
, obtained from the gluing data attached to
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
is a convex simplicial space.

To set up the geometry, we first recall from
[
77
]
.

Definition 5.1
.

The
convex sum
of two convex sets
A,B\subset\mathbb{R}^{N}
is

A+_{c}B=\{\lambda a+(1-\lambda)b\mid a\in A,\penalty\ b\in B,\penalty\ \lambda\in[0,1]\}.

The
one-point suspension

\Lambda A
of
A
is the convex set
A+_{c}\{\ast\}
where
\ast\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
is some point which is non-colinear with any
a\in A
.

The non-colinearity assumption is required such that, if
A=\Delta^{n}
is a
n
-simplex, then its one-point suspension
\Delta^{n+1}=\Lambda\Delta^{n}
is the
(n+1)
-simplex.

Suppose
\Sigma\cong CS
is the
PL cone
over
S
, then if
S
has equipped a simplicial decomposition by the graph
\Gamma
, then
\Sigma
has equipped a simplicial decomposition given by the on-point suspension
\Lambda\Gamma
. For instance, if
S=S^{2}
were the PL 2-sphere, then
\Sigma
is homeomorphic to the PL 3-disc
D^{3}
.

We shall focus on this case first. Let
\Gamma
be a
connected
2-graph.

Definition 5.2
.

Denote by
\eta\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
the unit, and
\emptyset=\mathbb{G}^{\emptyset}
the trivial decorated 2-graph. A
categorical state
associated to the one-point suspension
\Lambda\Gamma
, also referred to as a
cone functor
, is an additive measureable functor

\omega=\omega_{\Lambda\Gamma}:\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\rightarrow{\cal H}^{\emptyset}\simeq\mathsf{Hilb},

for which
\omega(\eta)\in\mathsf{Hilb}^{\text{f.d.}}
is of finite-dimension.

By definition,
\omega
comes with an underlying field
\underline{\omega}
of Hilbert spaces on
\ast\times\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}=\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}
, such that

\omega(\phi)=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}}^{\oplus}d\nu_{\Gamma^{2}}(\mathrm{z})\underline{\omega}_{\mathrm{z}}\otimes\phi_{\mathrm{z}},\qquad\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})

where
\nu_{\Gamma^{2}}
is another Haar measure
\mu^{\prime}_{\Gamma^{2}}
on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}
.

An infinite-dimensional Yoneda embedding.

One crucial fact to keep in mind is that the data
\underline{\omega}
does
not
itself determine a measureable field in general. Indeed, the space
{\cal M}_{\omega}\subset\coprod_{x}\underline{\omega}_{x}
of measureable sections is not specified.

However, we do have access to a
Yoneda embedding
, which in the context of
Remark
3.2
is a instance of the double Yoneda lemma (Thm. 4.1.2 in
[
167
]
).

Proposition 5.2
.

There is a fully-faithful embedding
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})^{\text{m-op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}),\mathsf{Hilb})
, where
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})^{\text{m-op}}
denotes the opposite algebra object in
\mathsf{Meas}
.

Proof.

The embedding takes a 2-graph state
\bar{\phi}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})^{\text{m-op}}
, linear dual to one
\phi^{\prime}\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
, to a measureable functor
(\omega_{\phi^{\prime}},\mu_{\Gamma^{2}})
of the form

\omega_{\phi^{\prime}}(\phi)=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma^{2}}(\mathrm{z})\bar{\phi}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{z}}\otimes\phi_{\mathrm{z}},\qquad\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})^{\text{m-op}};

(5.1)

see
Remark
5.1
.

The full-faithfulness is obvious by
Definition
3.5
: each natural transformation
\omega_{\phi}\Rightarrow\omega_{\phi^{\prime}}
correspond to a bounded linear operator
\beta:\phi=\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(H^{\prime X})=\phi^{\prime}
of measureable sheaves.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

We emphasize that, by
C^{\text{m-op}}
for a category
C=(C_{0},C_{1},\operatorname{id},\circ)
internal to
{\cal V}=\mathsf{Meas}
, it means the monoidal structure
\otimes
and the compositions on the measureable categories
C_{0},C_{1}
are reversed. On the other hand, for the 2-graph states, the direct image functors induced by the 2-
\dagger
structures on
\Gamma
are a priori
covariant
on
C_{1}\rightarrow C_{1}
in
{\cal V}
, but reverses the "internal" composition
\circ
. The unitarity property of
Definition
3.15
mixes both, and makes the *-operations into an
m-op
contravariant functor.

\Diamond

Remark 5.2
.

This embedding, and the formula (
5.1
), determines a
categorical pairing form

\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})^{\text{m-op}}\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}),\mathsf{Hilb})\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{eval}}\mathsf{Hilb},

(5.2)

which was used in
[
62
]
as a "duality evaluation" for
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
.
16
16

16

Such pairing functors, if Frobenius, was also used by
[
29
]
as part of the definition of a Hopf category. However, we will not be using that notion here.
This categorfies the pairing functional
\langle\Psi_{2}\mid\Psi_{1}\rangle=\omega(\bar{\psi}_{2}(U)\psi_{1}(U))
defined on the 3d Chern-Simons holonomies
\psi(U)
as constructed in §6.2 of
[
23
]
.

\Diamond

A perhaps unfortunate fact is the following.

Proposition 5.3
.

The embedding
\phi^{\prime}\mapsto\omega_{\phi^{\prime}}
(
5.1
) is
\mathrm{not}
essentially surjective.

Proof.

By
Definition
3.5
, a measureable natural isomorphism
(\omega,\nu_{\Gamma^{2}})\Rightarrow\omega_{\phi}
to one coming from a 2-graph state
\phi
consist of (i) a Haar measure equivalent to
\mu_{\Gamma^{2}}
, and (ii) a field of
\mu_{\Gamma^{2}}
-essentially bounded sheaf of invertible operators
K:\omega\rightarrow\phi
.

We know from
Proposition
3.1
that (i) is not problematic. On the other hand, if a sheaf of invertible operator
K
in (ii) exists, then
\omega\in{\cal V}^{X}
itself must be a measureable sheaf of Hermitian sections. The existence of
K
for all
\omega
means that
{\cal V}^{X}\simeq{\cal H}^{X}
are equivalent, which is of course not the case.

Indeed, in the language of sheaves
Remark
2.1
, (ii) says that we can find a field of bounded isomorphisms from
any
Hilbert
W^{*}
-module to a Hilbert
C^{*}
-module, which is not possible in general.
∎

This issue is a consequence of the infinite-dimensional nature of the categories involved; it will show up again later in
Proposition
5.6
.

5.1.2
Transition states; cylinder functors on
\Gamma^{2}\times[0,1]

Consider the following geometry. Let
\Sigma\cong S\times[0,1]
be a manifold diffeomorphic to the cylinder on
S
. Equip
\Sigma
with a PL structure
C:\boldsymbol{\Delta}\rightarrow\Sigma
which defines a homotopy between the given PL structures
\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}:\boldsymbol{\Delta}\rightarrow S\times\{0,1\}
on the two copies of
S
.

We now wish to define the categorical functional
\omega_{C}
associated to the cylinder graph
C
.

Definition 5.3
.

The categorical functional associated to the homotopy
C
, or simply a
cylinder functor
, is a unit-preserving additive measureable functor

\omega=\omega_{C}:\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}),

such that the target is once again a 2-graph state.

Let us spell out what this means. Keep in mind that
\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
are
disjoint
.

A priori, the data of this additive measureable functor
\omega_{C}
involves an underlying field
\underline{\omega}
of Hilbert spaces over
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}
, together with a
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
-family of measures
\{\nu_{{\mathrm{z}}}\}_{{\mathrm{z}}\in\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}}
on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}
, such that

\omega_{C}(\phi)_{{\mathrm{z}}}=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}}^{\oplus}d\nu_{{\mathrm{z}}}(\mathrm{z}^{\prime})\underline{\omega}_{{\mathrm{z}},\mathrm{z}^{\prime}}\otimes\phi_{\mathrm{z^{\prime}}},\qquad\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}),\penalty\ \bar{\mathrm{z}}\in\mathbb{G}^{\bar{\Gamma}_{1}}.

This is not enough, however, as general measureable functors
\omega_{C}
may not produce a 2-graph state. An additional requisite condition is the following: that for each Borel subset
U\subset\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
, the assignment

U\mapsto\int_{U}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma_{1}}({\mathrm{z}})\omega_{C}(\phi)_{{\mathrm{z}}}

defines a sheaf of
L^{2}
-sections
\Gamma_{c}(H^{X_{1}})
of a Hermitian vector bundle
H^{X_{1}}\rightarrow X_{1}
over
X_{1}=(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}},\mu_{\Gamma_{1}})
. This puts constraints on
\underline{\omega}
.

Prior to proceeding, we first introduce the following notion.

Definition 5.4
.

We say the Radon measures
(\mu,\mu^{\prime})
are a
disintegration pair
on
Y\times X
iff for each
Y
-family
\{\nu_{y}\}_{y\in Y}
of disintegration measures, there is a
X
-family
\{\nu^{\prime}_{x}\}_{x\in X}
of disintegration measures such that

\int_{Y}d\mu(y)\int_{X}d\nu_{y}(x)f(y,x)=\int_{Y\times X}d\lambda(y,x)f(y,x)=\int_{X}d\mu^{\prime}(x)\int_{Y}d\nu^{\prime}_{x}(y)f(y,x)

for all measureable function
f
on
X\times Y
. Here,
\lambda
is a measure on
Y\times X
which is obtained by "integrating"
\nu_{y}
against
\mu
, or "integrating"
\nu^{\prime}_{x}
against
\mu^{\prime}
.

The existence and uniqueness of disintegration pairs
[
129
]
(see also Thm. 23 in
[
117
]
and Lemma 2.3 in
[
131
]
) gives the following.

Proposition 5.4
.

We have a disintegration pair
(\mu,\mu^{\prime})
whenever

\mu(U)=0\implies\lambda(U\times X)=0,\qquad\mu^{\prime}(V)\implies\lambda(Y\times V)=0

for each measureable
U\subset Y,\penalty\ V\subset X
. In which case, they are unique.

Characterizing
\omega_{C}
and pairings along the cylinder.

Let us now try to characterize
\omega_{C}
on the cylinder under the assumption that the given Haar measures
(\mu_{\Gamma_{0}},\mu_{\Gamma_{1}})
form a disintegration pair on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}
.

For each Borel
U\subset\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
, we rewrite the direct integral of
\omega_{C}(\phi)
in the following way,

\displaystyle\int_{U}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma_{1}}({\mathrm{z}})\omega_{C}(\phi)_{{\mathrm{z}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{U}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma_{1}}({\mathrm{z}})\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}}^{\oplus}d\nu_{\mathrm{z}}(\mathrm{z}^{\prime})\underline{\omega}_{{\mathrm{z}},\mathrm{z}^{\prime}}\otimes\phi_{\mathrm{z^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma_{0}}({\mathrm{z}}^{\prime})\int_{U}^{\oplus}d\nu^{\prime}_{\mathrm{z}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{z})\underline{\omega}_{{\mathrm{z}},\mathrm{z}^{\prime}}\otimes\phi_{\mathrm{z^{\prime}}}\equiv\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma_{0}}({\mathrm{z}^{\prime}})(\Omega_{\mathrm{z}^{\prime}})_{/U}\otimes\phi_{\mathrm{z^{\prime}}},

which gives us a
\mathsf{Hilb}
-valued presheaf on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
,

\Omega_{\mathrm{z^{\prime}}}:U\mapsto(\Omega_{\mathrm{z^{\prime}}})_{/U}=\int_{U}^{\oplus}d\nu^{\prime}_{\mathrm{z}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{z})\underline{\omega}_{{\mathrm{z}},\mathrm{z}^{\prime}},\qquad\mathrm{z^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}

for each
\mathrm{z^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}
.

Recall from Lemma 4.3 of
[
168
]
that a
S
-family of sheaves on
X
is a sheaf on
X\times S
which is flat over
S
. We then have the following characterization.

Proposition 5.5
.

Suppose
(\mu_{\Gamma_{0}},\mu_{\Gamma_{1}})
forms a disintegration pair. Then
\omega_{C}(\phi)\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}})
is a 2-graph state for all
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}})
iff
\Omega
defines a
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}
-family of sheaves of finitely-generated projective
C(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}})
-modules of
L^{2}
-sections on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
.

Proof.

The hypotheses guarantee that the sheaf
U\mapsto\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma_{0}}({\mathrm{z}^{\prime}})(\Omega_{\mathrm{z}^{\prime}})_{/U}\otimes\phi_{\mathrm{z^{\prime}}}=\int_{U}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma_{1}}({\mathrm{z}})\omega_{C}(\phi)_{{\mathrm{z}}}
is well-defined, and that it is equivalent to a sheaf of sections of a Hermitian vector bundle over
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
by the Serre-Swan theorem
[
120
,
121
]
.

Conversely, suppose the above sheaf defines a 2-graph state for all
\phi
. Evaluating
\omega_{C}
on the unit,

\omega_{C}(\eta_{0})=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma_{0}}(\mathrm{z})(\Omega_{\mathrm{z}})_{/U}\otimes\eta_{0}\cong\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}}^{\oplus}d\mu_{\Gamma_{0}}(\mathrm{z})(\Omega_{\mathrm{z}})_{/U},

implies that
U\mapsto(\Omega_{\mathrm{z}})_{/U}
defines a sheaf. Since each stalk
(\Omega_{\mathrm{z}})_{\mathrm{z^{\prime}}}
is finitely-generated and projective as a
C(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}})
-module, so is the sheaf
U\mapsto(\Omega_{\mathrm{z}})_{/U}
by
Proposition
5.1
.
∎

By definition, measureable natural transformations between cylinder functors
\omega_{C},\omega^{\prime}_{C}
correspond to (
\mu_{\Gamma_{1}}
-essentially) bounded linear operators of sheaves on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
.

By leveraging this characterization, there are embeddings that can be written down.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
C:\Gamma_{0}\Rightarrow\Gamma_{1}
denote a homotopy between 2-graphs.

•

There are fully-faithful embeddings

1.

\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}})^{\text{m-op}}\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}),\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}))
,

2.

\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}),\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}))\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}})\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}})^{\text{m-op}},\mathsf{Hilb})
.

•

Neither of which are equivalences in general.

Proof.

•

We will explicitly construct the embeddings in the following.

1.

The goal is to construct a
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}
-family of sheaves of Hermitian
L^{2}
-sections on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
from a pair of 2-graph states
\bar{\phi}_{0}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}})^{\text{m-op}},\penalty\ \phi_{1}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}})
. Here we emphasize that
\bar{\phi}
is the
linear dual
, not the *-operations.

To do so, we use the monoidal product on
\mathsf{Meas}
in Thm. 50 of
[
26
]
. Consider a 2-graph state
\Phi=\phi_{0}\times\phi 1
on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
subject to the following conditions.

–

\Phi
is
factorizable
: we have
\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\Phi=\phi_{1}
and
\operatorname{pr}_{0}^{*}\Phi=\bar{\phi}_{0}
as sheaves along the projection functors (
2.1
), and

–

\Phi
is equipped with a bounded Radon measure
\lambda
on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
, for which the given Haar measures
\mu_{\Gamma_{0,1}}=\lambda\circ\operatorname{pr}_{0,1}^{-1}
are the corresponding pushfowards.

These surjective submersive projections make
(\mu_{\Gamma_{0}},\mu_{\Gamma_{1}})
into a disintegration pair.

Since projective modules are flat, the presheaf
\Phi_{\mathrm{z}}:U\mapsto(\Phi_{\mathrm{z}})_{/U},\penalty\ U\subset\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
is a
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}
-family of finitely-generated projective sheaves on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
, which defines a cylinder functor
\omega_{\Phi}
as desired.

The full-faithfulness is clear from definition: measureable natural transformations between cylinder functors of the form
\omega_{\Phi},\omega_{\Phi^{\prime}}
are precisely sheaves of (
\mu_{\Gamma_{1}}
-essentially) bounded linear operators
\Phi\rightarrow\Phi^{\prime}
.

2.

Now consider a cylinder functor
\omega_{C}
. Given its associated family of sheaves
\Omega
, the linear dual gives rise to a
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}
-family
\bar{\Omega}
of finitely-generated projective
L^{2}
-sheaves on
\mathbb{G}^{\bar{\Gamma}_{1}}
.

Now let
\phi_{0}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}),\penalty\ \bar{\phi}_{1}\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}})^{\text{m-op}}
, and denote by
\tilde{\Phi}=\phi_{0}\times\bar{\phi}_{1}
the associated factoriazable 2-graph state defined from along the canonical projections.

Given the Radon measure
\lambda
as above, we can then define a cone functor
\Omega_{C}
by

\Omega_{C}(\phi_{0}\times\bar{\phi}_{1})=\int_{\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\bar{\Gamma}_{1}}}^{\oplus}d\lambda(\mathrm{z},\mathrm{z}^{\prime})\bar{\Omega}_{\mathrm{z},\mathrm{z}^{\prime}}\otimes\tilde{\Phi}_{\mathrm{z},\mathrm{z}^{\prime}}\in\mathsf{Hilb}.

Once again, the full-faithfulness is clear: measureable natural transformations
\Omega_{C}\Rightarrow\Omega^{\prime}_{C}
of the form above are precisely bounded linear operators between families of sheaves
\Omega\rightarrow\Omega^{\prime}
.

•

Given
Proposition
5.4
, the reasons for the non-essential surjectiveness is the following.

1.

First, cylinder functors of the form
\omega_{\Phi}
comes from factorizable sheaves
\Phi
which are projective in both coordinates
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
, whereas the characterization
Proposition
5.5
only requires flatness along
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}}
.
17
17

17

Though any flat module over a Noetherian ring is projective, it is well-known that continuous functions
C(X)
over any manifold
X
, with
\operatorname{dim}X>0
, is not Noetherian.

2.

Second, cone functors of the form
\Omega_{C}
come from families of very well-behaved sheaves, while generically their underlying field of Hilbert spaces
\underline{\omega}
have no constraint. Thus the issue is the same as in
Proposition
5.3
.

∎

Remark 5.3
.

The composition of the embeddings in the above theorem gives a full-faithful functor

\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}})^{\text{m-op}}\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}})\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{1}})^{\text{m-op}},\mathsf{Hilb}),

(5.3)

which extends the categorical pairing form (see
Remark
5.1
) to disjoint homotopic graphs
\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
. In fact, it is clear that, if
\Gamma_{1}=v
is trivial, then under the equivalence
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\ast})\simeq\mathsf{Hilb}
this functor (
5.3
) reproduces precisely the Yoneda embedding in
Proposition
5.2
.

\Diamond

Consider the one-point suspension of the disjoint union
\Gamma_{0}\coprod\Gamma_{1}
. It is PL homeomorphic to two tetrahedra on
\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
identified at the cone point (a PL cylinder "pinched" at the centre), which is an
irregular point
in the stratification (see fig. 2 in
[
78
]
). This leads to the fact that the right-hand side of (
5.3
), ie. the cone functors
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma_{0}})\times\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\bar{\Gamma}_{1}})^{\text{op}},\mathsf{Hilb})
, being "too large": it contains geometries which are not cylinders. Irregular points are also undesireable from the lattice theoretic perspective
[
78
]
, as they lead to ambiguities.

5.2
Gauge *-invariance of categorical states

Recall from §
3.3
that
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\subset{\cal V}_{q}^{X}
, for each 2-graph
\Gamma
, is a right *-module over
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
. The categorical linear functionals, which are supposed to define states on the
physical
degrees-of-freedom, should therefore be
invariant
under
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
. Such notions are captured by
module functors
.

These are by now very well-known, specifically in the theory of tensor categories
[
63
,
169
,
138
,
40
]
.

Definition 5.5
.

Let
A,B
denote two
\mathbb{C}
-linear monoidal categories. We say
{\cal M}
is an
A
-module if it comes equipped with a functor
\rhd:A\times{\cal M}\rightarrow{\cal M}
and the module associator natural transformation
(-\otimes-)\rhd-\Rightarrow-\rhd(-\rhd-)
.

1.

An
A
-module functor

F:{\cal M}\rightarrow{\cal N}
is a functor equipped with natural transformations
F_{a}:F\circ(a\rhd_{\cal M}-)\Rightarrow(a\rhd_{{\cal N}}-)\circ F
, satisfying monoidal coherence conditions in
A
.

2.

Let
{\cal N}
be a
B
-module. A monoidal functor
f:A\rightarrow B
induces the
restriction of scalars
functor
f^{*}\times 1_{\cal N}:-\rhd-\mapsto f(-)\rhd-
, which turns
({\cal N},\rhd_{B})
into an
A
-module:
a\rhd_{f}n=f(a)\rhd n
.

We will also recall the notion of a
rigid dagger
category
[
170
]
.

Definition 5.6
.

Let
{\cal M},{\cal N}
be rigid dagger categories. A
rigid dagger functor

F:{\cal M}\rightarrow{\cal N}
is a functor equipped with natural isomorphisms

F^{\text{m-op}}\circ(-^{*})_{\cal M}\cong(-^{*})_{\cal N}\circ F,\qquad F^{\text{op}}\circ(-^{\dagger})_{\cal M}\cong(-^{\dagger})_{\cal N}\circ F

(5.4)

preserving the rigid duality data, and satisfying the obvious coherence conditions against the rigid monoidal structures of
{\cal M},{\cal N}
.

In fact, when the rigid duality is involutive, a rigid duality structure can be thought of as a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times B\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-module structure on
{\cal M}
. This gives the delooping
B{\cal M}
the structure of a coherent 2-
\dagger
structure
[
155
]
.

5.2.1
Invariant categorical linear functionals

Consider a PL continuous map
\Gamma^{\prime 2}\rightarrow\Gamma^{2}
between two 2-graphs, and denote by
h:\Gamma^{\prime 1}\rightarrow\Gamma^{1}
the induced PL continuous map on their 1-skeleta, which by definition is a functor of PL 1-simplex groupoids.

We construct a functor
h^{*}:\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{\prime 1}}
on the 2-gauge parameters by pulling back
h
, which is easily seen to be
strictly
monoidal

\displaystyle h^{*}(\zeta\cdot_{\mathrm{h}}\zeta^{\prime})

\displaystyle=h^{*}\big((aa^{\prime})_{v}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{e}(a_{v}\rhd\gamma^{\prime}_{e}}(aa^{\prime})_{v^{\prime}}\big)=(aa^{\prime})_{h(v)}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{h(e)}(a_{h(v)}\rhd\gamma^{\prime}_{h(e)})}(aa^{\prime})_{h(v)}

\displaystyle=(a_{h(v)}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{h(e)}}a_{h(v^{\prime})})\cdot_{\mathrm{h}}(a^{\prime}_{h(v)}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{h(e)}^{\prime}}a^{\prime}_{h(v^{\prime})})=h^{*}(\zeta)\cdot_{\mathrm{h}}h^{*}(\zeta),

\displaystyle h^{*}(\zeta\cdot_{\mathrm{v}}\zeta^{\prime})

\displaystyle=h^{*}\big(a_{v}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{e}}a_{v_{0}}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{e^{\prime}}}a_{v^{\prime}}\big)=h^{*}\big(a_{v}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{e\ast e^{\prime}}}a_{v^{\prime}}\big)=a_{h(v)}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{h(e\ast e^{\prime})}}a_{h(v^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=a_{h(v)}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{h(e)}\gamma_{h(e^{\prime})}}a_{h(v^{\prime})}=a_{h(v)}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{h(e)}}a_{h(v_{0})}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{h(e^{\prime})}}a_{h(v^{\prime})}=h^{*}(\zeta)\cdot_{\mathrm{v}}h^{*}(\zeta^{\prime})

for each (horizontally/vertically) composable
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
.

This monoidal functor
h^{*}
then induces a restriction of scalars, sending
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
-modules to
\mathbb{U}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{\prime 1}}
-modules. We can therefore introduce the following notion.

Definition 5.7
.

Suppose there is a PL continuous map
\Gamma^{\prime 2}\rightarrow\Gamma^{2}
, then a
measureable
\bullet
-module functor

F:\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{\prime 2}})
is a measureable functor
\omega^{h}=(\omega,\nu)
— with
\nu
a (
\operatorname{pr}_{2}
-filtred) measure on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}\times\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{\prime 2}}
— equipped with a measureable natural transformation

\omega_{\zeta}:\omega\circ(-\bullet\zeta)\Rightarrow(-\bullet h^{*}\zeta)\circ\omega

for all
\zeta\in\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
, such that the diagram against the module associator
\alpha^{\Lambda}
,

\hbox to354.92pt{\vbox to91.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 177.45795pt\lower-46.14008pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

commutes. Here
\cdot
denotes either the horizontal or vertical composition, depending on the composability of
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}
.

Explicitly, the natural transformation
\omega_{\zeta}
is the data of a field of bounded linear operators

(\omega_{\zeta})_{\mathrm{z}^{\prime},\mathrm{z}}:(\omega\Lambda_{\zeta})_{\mathrm{z}^{\prime},\mathrm{z}}\rightarrow(\Lambda_{h^{*}\zeta}\omega)_{\mathrm{z}^{\prime},\mathrm{z}},\qquad\mathrm{z},\mathrm{z}^{\prime}\in X=\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}},

with measureability class
\sqrt{(\nu\lambda_{\zeta})(\lambda_{\zeta}\nu)}

[
117
]
, where
\lambda_{\zeta}
is the measure on
X\times X
underlying
\Lambda_{\zeta}
. We will assume
\omega_{\zeta}
is invertible in the following.

By inducing
\Lambda_{\zeta}
from a pullback (see §
3.1.2
),
\lambda_{\zeta}=\delta
is the delta measure and
f\delta=f=\delta f\implies\sqrt{ff}=f
by Radon-Nikodym. Taking the PL continuous map
h
to be the identity, we recover the notion of "measureable module endofunctors" introduced in the appendix of
[
62
]
, through the model change
Remark
3.3
.

This gives us the following
invariance
property.

Proposition 5.7
.

Cone
\bullet
-module functors
\omega
are
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
-invariant, hence they descend to categorical states on the 2-Chern-Simons observables
\omega\in\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathcal{O}^{\Gamma},\mathsf{Hilb})
.

Proof.

Recall
{\cal H}^{\emptyset}\simeq\mathsf{Hilb}
. Consider the constant PL continuous map
\ast\rightarrow\Gamma^{2}
sending a point to the root
v\in\Gamma^{2}
of a 2-graph, which gives rise the same trivial map on the 1-skeleta
h:\ast\rightarrow\Gamma^{1}
. Since the point
\ast
is undecorated, the induced map on the decorated 1-graphs is the monoidal counit
h^{*}(\zeta)=\tilde{\epsilon}(\zeta)
in
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
(ie. the trivial transformation for all
\zeta
).

By
Definition
5.2
, the
\bullet
-module structure on cone functors
\omega\in\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathsf{Meas}}(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}),\mathsf{Hilb})
then reads

\omega_{\zeta}:\omega\circ(-\bullet\zeta)\Rightarrow(-\bullet h^{*}\zeta)\circ\omega=(-\bullet\tilde{\epsilon}(\zeta))\circ\omega\cong\omega.

where we have by definition
-\bullet{\tilde{\epsilon}(\zeta)}\cong-\otimes\mathsf{Hilb}\cong 1_{\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})}
for all
\zeta\in\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
.

Now given 2-gauge transformations can be written in terms of the
\bullet
-bimodule structure (
3.11
), the last statement follows immediately.
∎

This is a categorified version of the invariance condition, eq. (6.7) of
[
23
]
, for linear functionals in discrete Chern-Simons theory.
18
18

18

Note we do not require the monoidality of categorical linear funcitonals under the monoidal structure given by
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}
, since such functors decategorifies into an algebra map, which does not correspond to a state on a
C^{*}
-algebra.

5.2.2
*-functors and cointegrals for Hopf categories

Recall from §
3.3.3
that the *-operations give the cocategory
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
with a dagger *-structure (in which the duality is
not
necessarily involutive). The unitarity property stated in
Definition
3.15
then allows us to construct the duality data on
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
(specifically the evaluation measureable functors; see the appendix of
[
62
]
).

Remark 5.4
.

In the following, we will only focus on the property (
5.4
). This is because infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces do
not
have coevaluation maps that satisfy the snake equation against the canonical evaluation map, and hence any infinite-dimensional analogue of
\mathsf{Hilb}
will not be rigid. Indeed, evaluation module functors on
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
have been written down in the appendix of
[
62
]
using the *-operations, but it does not have coevaluations.

\Diamond

Focusing on the cone functors
\omega=\omega_{\Lambda\Gamma^{2}}
for clarity, we define the following.

Definition 5.8
.

A
measaureable (cone)
\bullet
-module *-functor
is a cone
\bullet
-module functor
\omega:\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathsf{Hilb}
equipped with invertible measureable
\bullet
-module natural transformations

\omega^{\dagger}:-^{\dagger}\circ\omega\Rightarrow\omega^{\text{op}}\circ-^{\dagger},\qquad\omega^{*}:\bar{\cdot}\circ\omega\Rightarrow\omega^{\text{m-op}}\circ\bar{\cdot}

such that the obvious coherence conditions against the *-module natural transformations
\overline{\phi\bullet\zeta}\cong\bar{\zeta}\bullet\bar{\phi}
are satisfied.

Denote by
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathsf{Meas}}^{\bullet,\ast}(\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}),\mathsf{Hilb})
the hom-category of such
\bullet
-module cone *-functors on
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
.

We shall assume these measureable natural transformations are invertible.

Let us now prove the categorification of eq. (6.8) in
[
23
]
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
\omega
be a measaureable (cone)
\bullet
-module *-functor, then there are natural measureable isoomrphisms

\overline{\omega(\phi)}\cong\omega(\phi^{*_{1}}),\qquad\omega(\phi)^{\dagger}\cong\omega(\phi^{*_{2}})

for each
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
, intertwining the *-operations
Definition
3.17
.

Proof.

To begin, by definition, for each
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}})
we have linear isomorphisms

\overline{\omega(\phi)}\cong\omega(\bar{\phi})\cong\omega(S_{h}\phi^{\dagger_{1}}),\qquad{\omega(\phi)}^{\dagger}\cong\omega(S_{v}\phi^{\dagger_{2}}),

where we have used the unitarity property
Definition
5.1
to rewrite
\bar{\phi}
in terms of the horizontal/vertical antipodes
S_{h},S_{v}
and the 2-dagger structures on the 2-graphs,

(\phi^{\dagger_{1,2}})_{\mathrm{z}}=\phi_{\mathrm{z}^{\dagger_{1,2}}},\qquad\mathrm{z}\in\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{2}}.

However, by definition of the *-operations in
Definition
3.17
, these 2-dagger structures are related to
-^{*_{1,2}}
up to an action of the
R
-matrices (as well as the invertible 2-
\dagger
intertwiner pair
\eta=(\eta_{h},\eta_{v})
). Due to the invariance property
Proposition
5.7
of cone
\bullet
-module functors
\omega
, these are trivialized whence we achieve the natural measureable isomorphisms as desired.
∎

5.2.3
Cointegrals for Hopf (co)categories

Equipped with the notion of
\bullet
-module functors, we can then concretely interpret the Haar measure
\mu
of a Lie 2-group
\mathbb{G}
. Recall that a
left-/right-cointegral
of a Hopf algebra
H
is a linear functional
\lambda_{l},\lambda_{r}:H\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
for which

(\lambda_{l}\otimes 1)\circ\Delta=\eta\circ\lambda_{l},\qquad(1\otimes\lambda_{r})\circ\Delta=\eta\circ\lambda_{r},

respectively, where
\Delta:H\rightarrow H\otimes H
is the coprodut and
\eta:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow H
is the unit.
\lambda:H\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is simply called a
cointegral
if it is both a left- and a right-cointegral.

A classic example of a Hopf algebra, which is
not
in general finite-dimensional (but finitely-generated as
C^{*}
-algebra), equipped with a cointegral is the (undeformed) compact quantum group
C(G)
of Woronowicz
[
22
]
for a compact semisimple Lie group
G
. It is given precisely by the Haar measure on
G
.

Let us now introduce the (co)categorical version.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
H
denote a Hopf cocategory internal to a symmetric monoidal bicategory
{\cal V}
. A
left-/right-cointegral
for
H
is an internal functor
\Lambda_{l},\Lambda_{r}:H\rightarrow I
into the discrete internal cocategory
I\leftleftarrows I
on the monoidal unit
I\in{\cal V}
, such that there exist natural transformations

(\Lambda_{l}\times 1)\circ\Delta\Rightarrow\eta\circ\Lambda_{l},\qquad(1\times\Lambda_{r})\circ\Delta\Rightarrow\eta\circ\Lambda_{r},

(5.5)

satisfying the obvious coherence conditions against the natural transformations
\Delta\circ m\Rightarrow(m\times m)\circ\Delta
witnessing the bimonoidal axioms.

We call
\Lambda_{l},\Lambda_{r}

strong
iff these natural transformations are invertible. We say
\Lambda:H\rightarrow I
is an
integral
iff it is both a left- and right-cointegral such that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(1\times\Lambda\times 1)\circ(\Delta\times 1)\circ\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(1\times\Lambda\times 1)\circ(1\times\Delta)\circ\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\eta\circ\Lambda\times 1)\circ\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\eta\times\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(1\times\eta\circ\Lambda)\circ\Delta}}

against the coassociator
(\Delta\times 1)\circ\Delta\Rightarrow(1\times\Delta)\circ\Delta
commutes.

We can now prove the following.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
\mu
denote an invariant Haar measure for the compact semisimple
19
19

19

By semisimple here, we mean that there exist a non-degenerate graded Killing form on the associated Lie 2-algebra
\mathfrak{G}=\mathfrak{h}\xrightarrow{t}\mathfrak{g}
of degree-1
[
171
,
14
,
12
,
19
]
.
Lie 2-group
\mathbb{G}
, then the direct integral
\int^{\oplus}_{\mathbb{G}}d\mu(-):\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathsf{Hilb}
is a strong cointegral for the geometric 2-graph states
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G})
.

Proof.

By
Definition
3.2
,
\mu
has a disintegration along the source map for which the pushforward
\sigma=\mu\circ s^{-1}
is itself an invariant Haar measure. This allows us to define the measureable functor
\int_{G}^{\oplus}d\sigma(-):\mathfrak{C}(G)\rightarrow\mathsf{Hilb}
which fits into the strict commutative diagram
